Shown on any same-origin navigation past 250 ms (a tap on a link, a form sent; hidden on pageshow/load/pagehide, and after 12 s if a script cancelled the navigation), while Wander fetches more and while Find searches. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-21wave.
- The cut (Will, 21 Oct: "simplicity is the ultimate sophistication; cut the number of decisions"; "do it"): the footer is the wordmark, the wave ('We recommend you wander.'), four short columns (Look · Collect · The Keys · The house, `FOOT` and `footer()` in views.js, `.ft-*`) and one line; About carries 'The papers of the house' (Owners, the share, Who hangs, the Copyright Office, Enterprise, the app, the guest book, the journal). Doors closed, routes kept: the Collection's views lose Stroll, the frame and Free and clear; the front's 'Other ways in' is Wander and the headset; the work page no longer offers a trade in kind (/swap/new by address); Friends no longer links the Hall of Patrons; the guide is Hints always (`GUIDE_SWITCH` = '', the inline script sets data-guide=hints); Wander's settings sheet is Sit a while alone (Pace, Sound and the guide rows are gone; stroll.js handlers are null-safe); Keeping is two tiers (the studio free, Keeping $15), above by letter. /stroll 301s to /wander (it had been missed and answered 404 on live). `minute()` in app.js (`app.locals.minute`): a minute's page cache for strangers on /makers and /ledger, keyed by the register's head seq, so any entry makes the page new.
- The phone walk, 21 Oct (as a stranger on the live house, after the cut): a stray `/div>` text at the top of Wander (a closing tag that had lost its `<` in stroll.js) is gone; Wander's rail is Hat · Bravo · Hang · Collect · Guide · Share · More, with Card, Study and the basket behind More (`[data-st-more]`, `.st-more`; `I.more`; a tap elsewhere folds it; basket.js appends into the box when present); the work page's act row is Hat · Bravo · Collect, and 'Study it on the easel' sits in the why line (the Study act wrapped to a second row on a phone); the hint under the plate speaks to the device (`.lot-hint-c` click/arrows, `.lot-hint-t` tap/swipe by pointer:coarse); the pay sheet's fine print reads from the form's `data-fine` ('$5 to the photographer; the house's share is on top.' with the fee on; the old 85% otherwise); wave.js shows only when the click or submit was not taken over by a script (checked after the other listeners), and pay.js hides it when the sheet opens.
- The album (Will, 21 Oct: "something like ShootProof: deliver photos to a client safely through a watermark and a download link; they can download without payment"; "the photographers of the world… when they go out and shoot for friends, can deliver an album; their friends can still say thank you for the work; people can finally thank their friends"; the first is Rachel Dunnam's thirtieth): `src/deliveries.js`. Use `delivery` (DEFAULT_USES, kind 'delivery', perpetual, no price; left out of `q.uses`, the trade's and the companies' lists, so it is never offered on a wall; backfilled to every maker at start). The maker's desk /account/deliveries (rail 'Deliveries'): title, the client's name and email (optional: one letter from the house), a note, closes in 7/30/90/365 days or never, the files (the original or a 2,400 px credited copy), tick the photographs (held and kept-back alike) → POST makes a licence per photograph (licensee the client's name, no email on the licence, `intended_use` 'Delivered: <title>'), granted free, ending with the delivery; the link /deliver/<token>; Close ends the licences. The client's door /deliver/:token (`.dv`, dark; `private, no-store`): the photographs, Download each (/d/<token>, counted as any licence's: DL.delivery 10), Download all (/deliver/:token/all.zip, each file marked and counted; ordered before /:slug), one photograph's page, 'A thank-you card' (the house's card to the maker: tips.js takes `via=<token>` so a kept-back photograph may be thanked for and the card returns to the album), the footer saying what every file carries. `delivery.sent` hidden on the register; the licences' entries say 'delivered'; a client is never named in public. Timers unref'd (the smoke suite hung on them). Next (Will): friends Keep a photograph into their own gallery from the album, then the holder's room sells the proof, Instax and prints with a smile; the maker's watermark and credit stay on every file either way; a price on an album (Checkout before the files); a PIN; proofing. Test: smoke 'deliveries'.
- The bag check (Will, 21 Oct: "everything has to pass and clear a test before it can come into the house; sniffed and inspected, quickly, a little TSA; like the New York Public Library or the Met: no weapons when you upload"; "makers' accounts are screened, so we're not giving that to everybody"): `src/bagcheck.js`. `check(file, {expect: image|audio|archive|papers|proof})`: (1) what it is by its first bytes, never its name or the browser's word (JPEG, PNG, TIFF, WebP; WebM, Ogg, MP4/M4A, MP3, WAV; zip; PDF), anything else turned away by its real name (a web page, a script, a program, an SVG, a PostScript file, a HEIC with 'export as JPEG'); (2) nothing hidden: a zip inside a picture (six-byte header so pixels are never mistaken), a script, PHP, a page, an iframe in the bytes, or more than 64 KB after a JPEG's end; (3) it decodes whole (sharp failOn error, a small full decode) and is a size a photograph can be (BAGCHECK_MAX_MP 400, BAGCHECK_MAX_SIDE 30000). `gate(expect, {json})` is the middleware after multer on every door (the vault, the masters desk, /account/works/one, the portrait, /verify, /yours/add, the claim's evidence, the cabinet, a tour's voice, /voices); `throwIfBad` guards the chokepoints inside (`registerWork`, `takeMaster`), so arrivals from the bucket are checked too. A refusal unlinks the file, answers in plain words (415, a page or JSON), is kept in `bagcheck` (where, who, ip, name, bytes, type, reason) and told on the register as `upload.refused` (hidden). Studio → Back office → The door (/studio/bagcheck; it was /studio/door until 28 Oct, which shadowed the Studio door and looped signed-out visitors): this week's counts, who kept bringing the wrong thing, the refusals. guardrails.js gains limiters on the public doors (`bring`: /verify, /yours/add, claims, the cabinet, 30 an hour; `ask`: deliveries, quotes, rights, feedback, 40 an hour). Test: smoke 'the bag check' (a page named .jpg, a program, an SVG, a zip in a picture, cargo after the end, a truncated file, a script in the bytes, a picture at the voice door, a .wmark at /verify, the vault's own refusal, the desk, never on the public register).
- The vault, fixed (Will, 21 Oct: "you don't see thumbnails, you can't exit, you can't see if it's done; I don't even know if it works"): the page's inline script had died on a syntax error since the arrivals rewrite (a `\/` and a `\.` inside the template string were eaten, so the regex read `/^image//`), so choosing files did nothing at all. Fixed (`\\/`), and the page now tells you: `#aStatus` ('N files chosen, X MB. Nothing has been sent yet' · 'Sending: n of N' · 'n held so far; m still being read' · 'The house is busy, still waiting' · 'N of N held. All in.'), '← Your studio' at the top, 'Open its record' on every held row. Guard: smoke 'every inline script compiles' pulls every inline `<script>` from the front, the collection, a work, Wander, the Keys, Yours, the vault, the studio and the masters desk and compiles each with `new Function`.
- Arrivals, reliable (Will, 21 Oct: all eighteen of Rachel's photographs were refused by the bucket's missing CORS rule; "we have to do this reliably; this is important"): (1) the house sets the bucket's CORS rule itself: `store.ensureCors([origin])` in storage.js (GetBucketCors; a rule with the house's origin, PUT/GET/HEAD, ExposeHeaders ETag; PutBucketCors when missing; 'ok' | 'set' | 'forbidden' | 'error: …'), run 10 s after start and six-hourly (`app.locals.ensureVaultCors`), the answer on /healthz `vault.cors`; needs s3:GetBucketCORS/PutBucketCORS on the key (Will: if /healthz says forbidden, either grant it or set the rule once by hand per docs/ARRIVALS.md). (2) Whatever the bucket says, the browser falls back through the house: PUT /…/arrivals/:id/body now takes the whole file for any vault (streamed to disk, short bodies refused, the multipart upload aborted, `arrivals.via='house'`), and the pump takes a via-house arrival from its local file without a download or a bucket delete; arrivals.js (client) turns `viaHouse` on at the first CORS refusal (the rest of the visit skips the bucket) and retries the file through the house; `onVia('house')` lets the desk say 'through the house (a little slower)'. Test: test/s3.js (set, idempotent, forbidden), smoke's arrivals (the body road).
- Taking in, faster (Will, 21 Oct: "it's so slow"; Instagram's trick: the user is free while the work goes on behind): (1) vaulting in the background: `works.vaulted` (0 → 1); `registerWork` writes the original to the local disk, fingerprints, deeds and registers it at once, and the copy to the bucket follows in `vaultPending()` (app.js; 50 ms after the insert, 20 s after start, every 30 s; retried until it lands; `cfg.vaultNow` keeps the old synchronous put for tests); `trimOriginals` never drops an unvaulted original; the mirror takes only vaulted work; /healthz `vault.unvaulted`. (2) The pump takes ARRIVALS_PARALLEL (2) at a time (`takeOne`), so a decode overlaps a download. Still serial per file: the bag check's decode, the fingerprint, the dhash decode; next, one decode shared by all three.
- The only copy (21 Oct, Will: "Did everything properly get uploaded?"; the eighteen came in through the house and every picture was a 500): a line from the synchronous days (`if (store.kind === 's3') fs.unlinkSync(dest)` at the end of `registerWork`) deleted each local original the moment it was registered, before `vaultPending` had copied it to the bucket, so fourteen records held no file anywhere. Now: the local copy stays (it is the cache, trimmed by budget, never while unvaulted); conservation never drops an unvaulted original to "fetch it again" (`onlyCopy` in check); `vaultPending` reconciles a missing local file with the bucket (`store.size`), else puts the work off view with the finding ('not on disk and never reached the vault') and lists it on /healthz `vault.lost` (with `lastError`, `lastOkAt`, `vaulting`, `originalsOnDisk`, `dataDir`); /img answers 404 for a missing original, never 500; and the same file sent again comes home to its lost record (`registerWork`: same sha256, vaulted 0, nothing on disk → the file takes its place, `cameHome`, back on view, vaulted), so the maker simply sends the files again. Test: `test/vault.js` (in npm test, against the in-memory S3). Rachel's eighteen must be sent again once this is live.
- Deliveries, next (Will, 21 Oct): a better way of sorting and choosing works on the deliveries desk (by arrival, by place or title, the last upload first, a tick-all for a batch).
- The vault's two stages, said plainly (Will, 21 Oct: "it allows me to leave the page before it's done"): Sent means the file is on the house's disk; Held means the house has read it. The page now says 'Sent n of N (on the house's disk) · held k so far' while sending, warns through the browser if you try to leave with files still unsent (`onbeforeunload` while `sending`), follows the house's reading from the first file (not after the last send), and after the last send says 'All N sent … You can leave now: your studio shows each work as it is held.' The Held box reads 'All N works are in the vault' only when every one is.
- The sender never replays (21 Oct, Will: "there was nothing, I saw fingerprints"): public/arrivals.js remembered every finished file by name, size and date and answered a second send of the same file with the old arrival's id at once, so the vault page showed the old records and nothing went up. `send()` now starts a finished file afresh (the memory resumes only a send cut short); the masters desk still skips sent files through `sentBefore`; a 401 on the body road reads as signed-out. Test: `test/arrivals-client.js` (the browser sender under node with a fake house; in npm test).
- The album, a present (Will, 21 Oct: "allow someone to wander, but they still own it"; "Watermark branding on the delivery; delivering a present, a work of art; this is the artist's work"): /deliver/:token opens with the Watermark wordmark (`.dv-logo`, the logo inverted on the dark wall), 'From <maker> · for <client>', the title large, a gift line (`.dv-gift`: made for you and yours to keep; each file carries the maker's name inside it, the way a print carries a signature), the note as a letter signed with the maker's first name (`.dv-note` blockquote; the desk's field is `.dv-notef`), then 'Wander through them' (`.dv-wander`, gold rule) beside Download all; every photograph opens Wander at itself; the footer signs 'Delivered with Watermark · Where great work is kept.' Wander in an album: `in=delivery:<token>` (`app.locals.deliveries.set(token)`: the album's photographs in its order while the link is live, held or kept back; `isDelivery` in stroll.js lets hidden works through `inSet` and `?at=`; a closed or unknown album redirects to its own door), the chip is the album's title with × back to it, the set carries `via` so the rail's Card posts /w/:slug/thanks with the album's token and falls back to the album's #thanks. The single-photograph page offers 'Wander from here'. Test: smoke 'deliveries'.
- The master leaves untouched, and a better file on top (Will, 21 Oct: "never compressing the image in a way that is ever noticeable; our images have to be the most beautiful digital prints from the master; if we can't do that we will fail"; "allow a higher resolution version to come in on top of a small one and replace"): (1) `marked()` in app.js re-encodes only share-size copies; a full-size delivery (Collect's original, the Reference, albums, seats) is the maker's bytes with the papers added losslessly (piexif EXIF, the XMP segment), never decoded and re-encoded, and carries no pixel mark; it is traced by `downloads.sha256` and XMP. The smoke test proves the delivered pixels equal the master's. (2) `app.locals.upgradeIfHeld(file, name, makerId)` (app.js, in the masters lane): a maker's vault matches an arrival to the maker's own works by dhash (same orientation, ≤ 6) and, when larger, makes it the master of that record through `replaceMaster` (slug, deed, licences, entries unchanged; `work.master` on the register; the local copy now stays as the cache); a smaller or equal file is declined with the record named (outcome 'smaller', 409); `takeWork` (accounts.js) calls it before `registerWork` and answers `note` 'Now the master of X: held at N megapixels'. (3) The vault's rule reads 'The master, never an export for the web' (a JPEG at full size and quality 100 with its profile; TIFF and PNG taken; the house never re-encodes the original). docs/HOUSE.md 'The master leaves untouched'. TIFF 16-bit at 60 MP is over the 250 MB door and the live box's memory: JPEG 100 is the master for now; DNG as the negative later. Test: smoke 'a better file on top' and the mark policy block.
- Twins (21 Oct): the eighteen full frames Will sent at 16:0x arrived on the build before `upgradeIfHeld` and were registered as new hidden works beside the small ones (533 → 551). `mergeTwins()` in app.js (a minute after start; `app.locals.mergeTwins`): per maker, a newer hidden work with no licence, wall or edition that matches an older work by dhash (≤ 6, same orientation) and is larger becomes that record's master (`replaceMaster` from a copy of its original) and is retired as status 'merged' (in no listing, never deleted; `work.merged` hidden on the register). Test: smoke 'a better file on top'.
- The album's files, and the cold (Will, 21 Oct: "we can't be sending 30 MB files to people; she needs to download a smaller file"; "a cute error page: oh, oops, Watermark caught a cold; this will work in a second"): (1) /deliver/:token/:slug/file is the 2,400-pixel copy (JPEG, the mark within, the papers inside, no credit strip: `marked({ share: true, strip: false })`), what every Download and Download all give; `?full=1` is the full file, the master's own pixels, offered as a quiet second door ('Full size', 'The full files (N MB) for printing') when the album allows it (`SIZES`: original = copy and full; large = copy only). The zip writes one file at a time (`await` the archiver 'entry'), never eighteen masters in memory at once. (2) `stampJpegBuffer` in provenance.js: piexif sees only the file's head (SOI and the APP segments plus a bare SOS); the EXIF segment is spliced in by Buffer, so a 60 MB master is never copied as a string; `marked()` uses it with no temp file. (3) The cold page: /offline is 'Oh, oops · Watermark caught a cold.' with Try again and a script that tries again by itself every few seconds (`[data-cold]`); sw.js now shows it (or the last copy) for any 502–504, including Render's own HTML 502 page, which the old content-type test let through; `V` = wm-2026-10-21cold. First-time visitors during a deploy still see Render's page: only a CDN in front (Cloudflare, Will's DNS) or no deploys can change that.
- As good as Lightroom (Will, 21 Oct: "this looks terrible; our image compression is shit"; "we need to be as good as Lightroom or Leica"): the delivered 2,400-pixel copy went derivative (JPEG 90) → decoded, the mark laid in (±3 on 8×8 blocks) → JPEG 92: measured on L1002685 against the master resized, 36.6 dB with the block grid on the face; the same resize at JPEG 94 alone is 42.6 dB. Now: (1) nothing is laid into the pixels of any file the house makes, walls or deliveries (`marked()` no longer embeds; `MARK.embed` stays for reading old files and tests); every file is traced by its bytes, its XMP and its Content Credentials, and a copy re-encoded elsewhere is a stranger: the price of untouched pixels. (2) `images.copy(original, 2400)`: a delivery copy straight from the master, Lanczos, sRGB, JPEG 94 at 4:4:4, mozjpeg, a Buffer; used by the album (`fileOf`) and by `deliver` for share copies (the strip still goes on a licensed share copy). (3) The walls: `Q` is JPEG 92/94/94 (screen/large/wall; thumb 80) and WebP 92/94/94 (thumb 76), renditions named `q3` so every copy is rebuilt, sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-21q3. The pictures gate passes. Rule kept: measure PSNR against the same resized pixels (the judge's own resize differs from Lanczos and hides the encode).
- Download all, at once (21 Oct, "not working": eighteen copies from 60 MP masters took the live box ten seconds each, so the zip trickled for minutes): the album's copy is the wall's own 'large' JPEG (now JPEG 94 from the master, the same recipe as `images.copy`), cached in the renditions and warmed when the album is made (50 ms after) and for every live album 90 s after start (`warmAlbums`, not under test), so Download all and each Download stream at once; the papers are added per download as before.
- Security, backend only (Will, 22 Oct: "nothing cheesy on the front end; straight up security measures in the backend"): /healthz answers the street with up/down, the register's soundness, counts (works, unvaulted, lost) and mail/Stripe mode only; the full report (paths, the vault's last error and lost slugs, mail state, Connect, backups, fixity, conservation) needs the Studio cookie or HEALTH_KEY (else DESK_KEY) as `x-health-key` or ?key=; /.well-known/security.txt (RFC 9116: HOUSE_EMAIL, a year's expiry, the claims page); `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs `npm ci`, `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` and `npm test` on every push (Will turns on Render's "Wait for CI"). The standing measures, Will's hands (key rotation, least privilege, VAULT_KEY in the password manager, 2FA, the mirror, Cloudflare, an uptime monitor) and the next code are in docs/SECURITY.md. The same-origin check on writes already stood (guardrails.js `sameOrigin`).
- The morning of the Magnum night (Will, 23 Oct: "the digital Library of Alexandria"; "too much, endless scroll; people like the full-width bands"; "the Frick: the door as beautiful as the masterpiece; no waste"): (1) The front door is the hero, Wilhelm's three doors, the wings (`wings()` in views.js: a full-width band per place, six on the front, 'Every wing' to /collection#wings) and Lately; the information desk, the shows/walls/collections bands, For you, the Great Rooms band and the 'by name' row left the front (all still on /collection and in the footer); the 'We recommend you wander' line shows only when there is no hero. (2) The work page folds: the thank-you card (`.ty-fold`), the guest book (`.nt-fold`, open after signing) and Provenance and record (`#record`) are `details.lot-fold` closed by default, opened by hash (a script on the page); the Hang it explainer is one line (`.hg-why`); the claim door sits in the record. (3) The photographer's first minute on a phone (walked with Playwright at iPhone size against a local house, scratchpad walk.js): the vault's chooser comes before the rules on a phone (`.vt` grid; rules beside on a desk), the arrival row wraps (`.vt-s`), the deed line spans the row; the maker's work row (`.hd-row.ac-row-bt`) is laid out for a phone with the stars shown so grading and publishing work from the gallery floor; a one-time 'Swipe up for the next' nudge in Wander (`.st-swipe`, `wm_swiped`); every maker-facing '85%' reads from `FEE.yours()`. (4) The cold page's frame carries the swimming wave (`WMWave.show(.nf-blank)`). test/vault.js reads the brief /healthz (`app.locals.vaultState`). Stripe Checkout is titled 'Krafftco LLC': the public business name in Stripe is Will's to set to Watermark.
- It's yours (Will, 23 Oct: "instant gratification, like you've just received that Instax postcard"): `thanks()` in views.js for a personal piece (use social|reference) is the instant-film tile (`.yx`, `.yx-tile`, the picture developing by `yx-dev`, the number in pencil `.yx-pencil`, the wave `.yx-wave`), the money in a line each (`FEE.makerOf`), Save the photograph · Your gallery · Wander on, the proof and the papers. Every road lands there: Checkout (/thanks/:ref?session_id=), the pocket one-tap and the wallet's return (/thanks/:ref?k=<download_token>, `byKey`), the payment in place (`handover().thanks`; pay.js `done` goes there). The pocket's one tap is a choice: `buyers.pocket_tap` (default 1; POST /pocket/tap; the switch in `pocketBlock`); `pocket.tap(b)` is the balance a one-tap may use and `spend` refuses when off, so every Collect and card goes through the pay sheet (Will's demo wanted Apple Pay to show). Test: smoke (the pocket Collect lands on the tile; a wrong key is sent to the license page).
- Wander in the subway (Will, 23 Oct: "no downtime; like Spotify; lost connection, restored connection; people come here all the time and wander"): the pack in public/stroll.js (`PACK_N` 40, `wm_pack` in localStorage {at, items}, `packUp` 8 s after load and when the pack is empty, `warm` fetches each picture through the app's cache one every 900 ms, refreshed every six hours); `more()` on a failed feed hangs `fromPack`, `lost()`/`found()` say 'No connection. Wandering what you brought with you.' / 'Connected again.' (also on the online/offline events); opened with no signal, the inline screens are replaced by the pack. sw.js keeps one copy of /wander (network-first, key '/wander', before the PRIVATE check); the feed and the drawers stay uncached. Deploys still restart the disk-backed service (~1 min 502): returning visitors are covered by the app; strangers need Cloudflare in front (Will's DNS) or no deploys while people arrive.
- The dashboards (Will, 23 Oct: "the back of house as beautifully designed as the front; 21st-century perfect; anybody could manage it"; later, after the Magnum night): the desk, one screen: the line (name, today, this month, what needs you), three columns (Needs you with one verb a row · The work · Money), everything else by name in one menu; one shell for maker, gallery, institution and the house, the role deciding the rows; no paragraphs on the desk; `/` finds anything. Build the maker's desk first behind the same addresses, then the back office.
- Pinch inside Wander (Will, 23 Oct, with the Duo in hand: "you need to be able to zoom and pinch inside Wander"): in public/stroll.js (`Z`, `zApply`, `zClamp`, `zEnd` on the scroller's touch handlers, ahead of the side doors): two fingers scale the photograph about the pinch (1–5×), one finger pans within the screen, a tap or a pinch back under 1.08 sets it to rights, moving on resets; while zoomed `.st-zoom` on the room holds the scroll (overflow hidden, snap off, touch-action none) and rests the chrome. The browser's page zoom never fires. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-23pinch.
- Print on Instax, and the tour (Will, 23 Oct, for the Magnum night: "an instant Instax print button"; "show people how to use the app"): the thanks tile (`.yx-acts`) carries 'Print on Instax' (`.yx-instax`: /yours/p/:ref/instax?film=mini&k=, with `download`, so save.js hands the Instax-sized file to the phone's share sheet → Save Image → Photos → the Instax or Leica FOTOS app prints it; save.js `isFile` now matches `/instax`). /start (`startPage` in views.js): Wilhelm's one-minute tour in three columns (look · collect · make) with the house's glyphs; footer Look → 'How it works'.
- Watermark Publishing (Will, 24 Oct: "let's start building out the Library of Alexandria"; "start importing books of public record and make the most beautiful viewer"; "we need to make Krafft Publishing or Watermark Publishing"; "books and photo books, for sale and also available"): (1) The canon: `CANON` in reading.js (60 books a curious person is handed first); `find(title, author)` asks Gutendex by title and surname and takes the author's own best-taken English plain-text edition (never a number guessed); `shelveCanon()` brings them in one every 1.5 s in the background (`job` {on, at, done, missed}); Studio → The public domain: 'Shelve the canon', each shown as it lands, the misses named. (2) The reader as a book: the cover first (`.rdr-cover`: the imprint, title, author said as a person says it (`byName`), the line, chapters and minutes, Begin or 'Go back to your place', the edition line), a progress hairline (`.rdr-prog`), Pages | Scroll (`pref.mode`, Pages by default on touch: the chapter set in columns one screen tall with scroll-snap, a spread of two on a wide screen, turned by a tap at the edges, a swipe or the arrows; `turn()`), an ornament (❦) at a chapter's end, a colophon on the last; `.rdr [hidden]` is hidden. The theme toggle matches `button[data-rd-theme]`, never `html[data-rd-theme]`. (3) The imprint /publishing (`.pub-*`; `.pb` is the plate's Collect mark): three shelves (the public domain free; photo books; editions) with cover cards, 'Publish with the house' by invitation; footer The house → Publishing. (4) Photo books: `texts.plates` (JSON [{slug, note}]; column added in library.js), kind 'photobook' (Library KINDS), made from a wall at Studio → The public domain ('A photo book from a wall': POST /studio/publishing/photobook: the wall's held works in order with their lines, the first the cover, a price or free, 'All rights reserved'); read in the room a plate at a time (`.rdr-pl`), 'Open the book' on the Library page. Sale: a priced photo book sells through the Library's take as any text (a PDF to deliver is still the house's to set); next: a PDF made from the plates, the maker's own publishing desk, Standard Ebooks editions. Test: smoke 'the public domain and the reading room'.
- The Bookshop (Will, 24 Oct: "the best bookstore in the world; better than the Strand; a place for book lovers, for first editions, signed copies, prints and reprints, and for supporting the artist directly"): `src/bookshop.js`. `copies` (text_id, kind first|signed|reprint|paper|proof|other, seller_kind house|maker|buyer + seller_id, line, condition, note, price_cents (the seller's), qty, photo_slug (a held work as the photograph of record), attest, status offered|sold|withdrawn), `copy_orders` (ref CB-YYYY-NNNNN, address, cents = price + FEE.premium when the fee is on, house_cents, paid, status asked|packed|sent|done|declined), `wants` (email, kind; told once by mail when such a copy is offered). The book's page (/library/:slug) carries `block`: the copies offered with Buy it, shipped (name, email, US address → Checkout, metadata.copy_order → `app.locals.bookshopEvent` on checkout.session.completed → `settle`: paid, qty down, buyer and seller mailed, `copy.sold` on the register without names) and the want; /bookshop: the rare shelf, in paper, the Library's books; /bookshop/o/:ref the buyer's page; Studio → Bookshop (/studio/bookshop): offer a copy (for the house, a maker by handle or a collector by code), the orders moved Asked → Packed → Sent → Delivered (the buyer mailed each step), the shelf, the wants; POST /account/bookshop/offer and /yours/bookshop/offer let a maker or collector offer their own (attest=1; no page for it yet). 'First edition' and 'signed' are the seller's attestation, said so on the page. Footer The house → The Bookshop; /publishing links it. Not yet: the house's reprint (a PDF/print-on-demand from the reading room's text), a seller's own desk, an appraiser, counsel on consignment. Test: smoke 'the Bookshop'.
- Set beautifully (24 Oct): `polish()` and `titleCase()` in reading.js at bring: straight quotes curl, `--` → em dash, `...` → …, `_word_` → *word* (the reader sets `*…*` as <em>), shouted heads (CHAPTER IV. THE RIVER) in title case with small words down and numerals up, a line of asterisks a section break (`* * *`, shown as ⁂).
- Wander across everything (Will, 24 Oct, after StumbleUpon: "add what is helpful and useful"): in the open wander (no word, maker or set) `textScreen()` in stroll.js sets a page from the shelf among every page of screens: a public book with a body, a chapter at random, its first paragraphs (~520 chars), as `{kind:'text', slug:'t:<book>:<c>', title, author, head, excerpt, href:/read/<book>?c=<c>, n}`; the server's `textCard` and the client's `screenFor` draw it (`.st-i.st-txt`, `.st-tx`: From the shelf, the title, the author, the chapter, a drop cap, the text fading at 46svh, Read on); no rail, no plate, no eye; `stood` never posts for a `t:` slug. The reading room's Pages mode is the viewer the house builds on. Next: plates from photo books and film stills as screens; the swipe-away that teaches For you; interests ticked once (the wings and the shelves). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24shelf.
- The book on the Duo (Will, 24 Oct: "the most beautiful, intuitive and easy; on the Duo you should feel like it is a book and swipe"): Pages gives a spread from 820px (the Duo open) with a gutter down the middle (`.rdr-paged .rdr-page:after`), running heads (`.rdr-head`: the title left, the chapter right) and a running foot with the page count ("Chapter 1 of 2 · p. 1 of 13", `[data-rd-foot]`, from the column widths); a tap at either edge turns, in the middle brings the chrome back; two fingers set the type size (read.js touch handlers); the cover sits side by side from 820px. The canon (first run, 24 Oct, 2:45–2:58 pm): 51 shelved on the live house; the title check forgives an article and a possessive; three titles named as Gutenberg names them (Essays, First Series; The Varieties of Religious Experience; Tao Teh King). Next: a ribbon bookmark, read aloud in the house voice, 'Read next' on the colophon, the half-fold as two panels.
- The printing house (Will, 24 Oct: "the library Benjamin Franklin would build if he had this technology"; "allow other printing-press studios to make their own pages, and we get a cut of all of it; Krafft Studios' page is the template; everybody becomes their own media company under our brand"): `src/press.js`. Franklin's parts, mapped: the vault and the walls (pictures), the reading room and the Library (books), the Bookshop (copies), the register (the record), notices and letters (the post), Friends and Keeping (the subscription library), the Keys (the Junto). New: the writing desk /account/writing (rail More → Writing): a maker publishes an essay, journal, book, history or paper (title, kind, a line, the text in the house's paragraph form, a price or free, a cover from their own works, shown or not) → a `texts` row under `maker_id`, author the maker's name, terms '© name. All rights reserved.', `text.shelved` from:'press' on the register; edit, take down / put back. The maker's page carries `press.block(m)`: Writing · Books (photo books) · From the shelf (copies they offer), as card rows (`.pw-sec`, `.pw-card`); the reading room sets a maker's text as 'Published by the house'; Wander's shelf screens draw maker essays too. The cut: the Library's take charges the maker's price plus FEE.premium when the fee is on (`total` in library.js; the maker's side of text sales is not yet on the payouts statement: paid by hand, next). /press: the printing house as the public reads it (the six parts, a count of makers who have published); footer The house → The printing house. Test: smoke 'the printing house'.
- The press itself (Will, 24 Oct: "an app as revolutionary as the Gutenberg press"; "one-person media companies with royalties"): (1) `src/edition.js`: /read/:slug.pdf sets any text into a printable book block (pdfkit; 5.5 × 8.5 in; a title page with the imprint, each chapter on its own page with its number in small capitals, justified text with first-line indents, running heads (title left, chapter right), page numbers, a colophon with the book's address); free texts to anyone (cached an hour), a priced text to its maker, the Studio or a paid holder (?t=token or the signed-in buyer); linked from the reading room's colophon and the Library page; mounted before /read/:slug. Set in Times until the house's fonts are on disk (Newsreader/Instrument Serif come from Google). (2) Royalties: when the house sells a reprint or copy of a living maker's text through the Bookshop, `payouts` (kind 'royalty', owed) credits the maker REPRINT_ROYALTY_PCT (50, draft) of the copy's price; `royalty.owed` hidden on the register. (3) The maker's statement (payouts.js) now carries Writing sold (the maker's price whole when the fee is on) and Copies from your shelf, in what is owed. Next: the house's fonts on disk for the press; the reprint as a Bookshop offer made automatically for every public-domain book (a price, the house prints and ships); a seller's own desk.
- Al (Will, 24 Oct: "make it Al, the AI, instead of Wilhelm; a little Einstein"; "he's there to help, guide, lead; Professor Einstein, here to teach and help you discover things; then Leonardo and Franklin, the best thinkers, spotlighted"): the house's guide is Al in every line of copy (Al asks · Al says · Al, at the door · read by Al · Read by Al, the house's AI · Al's three studies; the stroll's guide sheet 'Look closer · Al'); `I.wilhelm` and the `.ic-wil`/`.st-wil`/`.gc-wil`/`.kd-wil` classes keep their names in code. The glyph is redrawn to the brief: hair up and out in every direction, round glasses, a moustache, a bow tie; an original figure, never a likeness (viewBox 2 2 44 46). Next: the thinkers spotlighted (Franklin's shelf exists; a Leonardo shelf: the Notebooks on Gutenberg; a room per thinker with their books, what Al says about them, and the house's works that rhyme). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24al.
- Zoom that holds, and Hold (Will, 24 Oct: "zooming is not happening on Wander; it has to be the double tap for the hat"; "a toggle that comes on and off that shows if you are locked and in full screen; that could be if you are drawing"): in public/stroll.js the pinch now stays where the fingers leave it (`zHold`; up to 6×; one finger pans; a pinch back under 1.08, a double-tap or moving on sets it right), a double-tap zooms in 2.5× on the spot (`zTap`) and the hat is on the rail only (the two lifts of a pinch had read as the double-tap), a pointerup within 400 ms of a pinch or a pan is never a tap, and Safari's own gesturestart/change/end are a second road (the page's zoom refused). Hold: `[data-st-hold]` in the bar (L; Esc releases): the browser's full screen, the wake lock, nothing scrolls or swipes (`.st-holding` on the room: overflow hidden, touch-action none; one-finger touches prevented), the chrome away, and the badge `.st-hld` top centre ('Held · full screen' or 'on this screen'), which is the way out; leaving full screen by the system's own way releases the hold. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24hold.
- The rail that gets people to act (Will, 24 Oct: "make the buttons make sense and make them really powerful to get people to do things; buttons in Wander don't get people to use"): verbs, tiers, and one gold act. Wander's rail (src/stroll.js `card` and public/stroll.js `screenFor`, the same markup) is Hat · Bravo · Save (was Hang) · Look closer (was Guide) · Draw (the easel, `I.pencil`, up from More) · Share · More (Thank, was Card; Sit a while, which left the top bar; the basket) · Collect, last and nearest the thumb as the only filled button (`.st-buy`: gold, 'Collect' over the price, 'Yours to keep' beneath). Every button carries a title saying what it gets you; the first Save explains itself once (`wm_save_told`). Found on the way: `.st button{padding:2px 1px;background:none…}` (the studio's star form, line ~1472) had been matching `main.st` and flattening every rail button for days; it is `form.st` now. The Hold badge is `.st-hld` (`.st-held` is the rail's Yours). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24rail.
- Prime time (Will, 24 Oct: "do whatever makes the application best and ready for prime time"): the rail is five acts and one more: Hat · Bravo · Save · Share · More · Collect (gold); More opens a three-column grid (`.st-more`): Look closer (when there is a guide) · Draw · Thank · Sit · Basket. The top bar: Watermark · Find · Hold · ×. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24rail3.
- The shelf (Will, 24 Oct, an auction house's advertisement for rare books: a dark wall, three shelves, the books standing as objects with their shadows, one line in gold, one act; "learn from this"): `src/shelf.js` (`band({ books, href, k, line, act })`, `book(t, href)`), `.shf-*` in style.css. A book with a cover photograph stands as itself; one without is bound in cloth (a colour from its title, the title and author in gold on the board, a rule above and below). Leads /bookshop ('Sell with the house.' → #sell) and /publishing ('Publish with the house.' → #publish); fewer than five books make one shelf. Next: the front door's Lately or a 'From the shelf' band; the shelf as a Wander screen.
- The Listening Room (Will, 24 Oct: "what Creative Commons works can we use to make a listening room"; "let's make it happen; do it now"): `src/listening.js`, `public/listening.js`, `.lr-*`. The Keys' room for sound, after the Screening Room's rules. Three layers must clear (composition, recording, performance); the door (`clear(meta)`): public-domain marks and CC0; CC BY / BY-SA / BY-ND played whole with the credit line; NC turned away ('a members' room inside a house that sells work'); US sound recordings published through PD_RECORDING_YEAR (the year − 101: 1925 in 2026, the Music Modernization Act's schedule) from the Great 78 Project (collection georgeblood/78rpm) are public domain; later 78s refused by year. Brought in from the Internet Archive by identifier or address (`bring`: the metadata API; `tracks`: the mp3 over the m4a over the ogg over the flac, in track order, lengths read; the files stay at the Archive and are handed to <audio> as they are, as the Screening Room hands a master to <video>; CSP media-src allows archive.org) or searched (`search`, advancedsearch, mediatype:audio, each result read at the door). `recordings` (one row per record: the files JSON, licence, credit line, the sleeve the house writes, chosen by / why, a cover from a held work or the Archive's image, status shelved | playing, plays). /listening: On the turntable (`tonight()`, deterministic by day), shelves The shellac · The open repertoire · From the makers (a maker's master: source 'maker', keyholders; the vault road is next), the rules; /listening/:slug: the platter turning while it plays, the arm on the disc, the sides through in order, a tap lifts the needle, space plays/pauses, the sleeve, the credit and the licence. Studio → Programme → Listening Room (/studio/listening): search, bring in, the sleeve, set playing. Register: `recording.playing` public ('X is on the turntable in the Listening Room'), `recording.shelved` hidden. The Keys card opens (/listening). Sources worth the first evening: Musopen, the Open Goldberg Variations and the Open Well-Tempered Clavier (CC0), the Great 78 Project through 1925, the LoC jukebox. Counsel reads the door's rule before it is relied on. Test: smoke 'the Listening Room'.
- The first evening in the Listening Room (Will, 24 Oct: "load it up with the stuff that lasts; the work people should listen to, that helps their brain and mind"): `FIRST` in listening.js, fifteen records the house trusts without a second look (Ishizaka's Open Goldberg, WTC Book I and Art of Fugue; Musopen's complete Chopin and Figaro; Great 78 discs through 1925: Rachmaninoff playing Chopin and his own prelude, Caruso 1908/1909, King Oliver with Armstrong 1923, Bessie Smith 1925, Kreisler 1916, Paderewski 1924, Whiteman 1920, Sousa 1919), each with a line (drafts), brought in by `seed()` forty seconds after start when the shelf is empty (one every 2.5 s; not under test; NO_SEED=1 skips) and set playing. The door now marks 'licenses/publicdomain' and 'choose/publicdomain' as the uploader's word (`trust: 'uploader'`; the desk says 'check it'): many such items at the Archive are modern commercial recordings mislabelled, so the house looks before it plays them. Identifiers were checked against the Archive's search on 24 Oct.
- No lock on Wander (Will, 24 Oct: "make sure there's not a lock on it, because that lock is confusing"): the Hold button is gone from the bar; the bar is Watermark · Find · ×. The hold itself stays reachable by L on a keyboard (for drawing at a desk), and the badge shows only then. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24nolock.
- Tightened (Will, 24 Oct: "let's tighten everything up"; "are those songs too cheesy"; "what would the Library of Alexandria do"): an audit walk of twenty-eight public doors at phone and desk size (scratchpad audit.js: status, overflow, script errors, failed requests) found one fault: Wander on a desk asked for `/undefined` (the prefetch and the offline pack reaching for a shelf screen's picture); both now skip a screen with no picture. The Listening Room's first evening reads as a person says it (`NAMES` in listening.js, applied by `tidySeed()` at start and after a seed: 'The Goldberg Variations · Kimiko Ishizaka · J. S. Bach', never the Archive's file-name titles); the two that were cheer rather than canon (Sousa; Whispering) are taken off the turntable (`CUT`), and four that belong are added (Beethoven's Fifth, a Musopen recording; Melba and Caruso 1907; Heifetz's Schubert 1917; Ponselle's Brahms 1924); `seed()` now brings in whatever of FIRST is missing, not only an empty shelf. Alexandria's answer, as the house reads it: keep the whole of what exists, make the authoritative edition of each, and catalogue it (Callimachus's Pinakes); the turntable is the edition, the shelves the catalogue, and the record that is merely popular is kept but not put on. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24tight.
- The rows (Will, 24 Oct: "rather than show the entire list of everything, order it better; a better presentation, like Spotify's playlists; the most beautiful buckets and folders; the quickest, easiest menu to navigate; what if Apple TV was a museum"): `src/rows.js` (`rowsFor(req, data)`, `html`), `public/rows.js`, `.rw-*`/`.rws`. The front door under the hero is rows of large tiles on the dark, each row a bucket, scrolling sideways, a name and one line on each tile: Where to? (Wander · Your gallery · For those who make · Today, wide) · On view (shows, then walls) · The wings (the rooms by place with their best work) · The makers (portraits) · <slot> in the Keys (the clock's programme) · The Listening Room (discs) · The Library (books, 2:3) · The Screening Room (when a film is showing). Arrow keys move tile to tile and row to row, Enter opens, a hover or focus brings a tile forward; `collection({ rowsHtml })` shows the rows in place of Al's doors, the today band, the wings and Lately (all still at their own doors; `rowsHtml` null restores the old front). The test 'today on the front door, in the rows'. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24rows.
- The ends (Will, 24 Oct: "keep on perfecting every experience"): the end of a book and the end of a record. Reading room: 'Read next' on the colophon (`readNext` in reading.js: two other books as objects, the shelf's cloth, the pair chosen by the day); the ribbon (R, or the Ribbon button in the bar; `wm_read_<slug>_rib`): lays a red ribbon at the place, shown on the chapters list and offered on the cover as 'Go to the ribbon' (`.rdr-ribg`, `.rdr-ribbed`, `.rdr-cover-rib`). Listening Room: after the last side the next record on the shelf is offered (`nextOf` → `window.__LR.next`; `.lr-nextrec`) and after twelve seconds played (`?play=1`; a tap goes at once; Esc stays), so the room carries on as a radio does. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24ends.
- Why the pinch kept failing in Wander, and the fix (Will, 24 Oct: "why is zoom always broken on Wander but works on Study"): the easel's stage is `touch-action: none`, so the browser never owns a touch there; Wander's scroller was `touch-action: pan-y`, so the moment the first finger moved a few pixels the browser began a native scroll, and when the second finger landed the pinch was already lost (touchmoves non-cancelable, or touchcancel). Now on a coarse pointer the scroller is `touch-action: none` (style.css) and Wander scrolls itself (public/stroll.js `OWN`): one finger moves the wall with the finger (snap off during the gesture), and `settle(dy, dt)` goes one screen on or back when the drag passes 18% of the screen or 0.45 px/ms, else stays; native snap is handed back once it arrives; wheel, keys and the arrows are untouched. Two fingers at any moment are the pinch, as on the easel. Proved with synthetic touches (scratchpad swipe.js, pinch2.js). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24own.
- Iron (Will, 24 Oct: "it still doesn't work and is getting stuck; find the bug and rebuild"): two bugs. (1) A pinch whose second finger landed on the rail, the plate's links or the eye was refused (`closest('button, a, input')` on the two-finger touchstart): on a phone the rail covers the right edge, so a right-handed pinch often failed. Two fingers are a pinch wherever they land. (2) A held zoom had no way out but a pinch back or a double-tap: one finger only panned, so the wall was stuck. Now a flick (70 px at 0.6 px/ms) or a drag that pushes past the picture's edge (`Z.pan.stuck` > 60 px, measured from what the clamp ate) lets the zoom go and moves one screen on; a slow drag still pans; the top bar stays usable while zoomed; the first hold says once 'Zoomed. One finger moves around; a flick, a double-tap or a pinch back lets go.' (`wm_zoom_told`). Proved with scratchpad flick.js. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24iron.
- The Listening Room, hi-fi (Will, 24 Oct: "as good as a high-fi listening room, the best in Japan; let's be Sonos; SoundCloud, Spotify, Apple Music, Sonos; we are the best"): (1) Lossless: `tracks()` keeps the FLAC beside the light file (`flac`); the room's Best | Light switch (`[data-lr-fi]`, `wm_lr_fi`; shown only when the record has a lossless file; 'Best · lossless' when the browser plays FLAC) reloads the side at the same second. (2) The phone's own controls: Media Session metadata (the side, the performer, the record and the room, the cover as artwork), play/pause, previous/next, seek, and the position state, so the lock screen and the car show the record. (3) Gapless sides: the next side is loaded while this one plays (`warm`, a second Audio with preload auto). (4) Wander with it (`[data-lr-wander]`, W): the house's pictures on the wall while the record plays (Wander in a same-origin iframe over the room, `.lr-wall`; the CSP's frame-ancestors is now 'self' and X-Frame-Options SAMEORIGIN) with a small bar (`.lr-mini`: play/pause, the side, The room). (5) Still: after eight seconds without a movement the words dim to the record (`.lr-still`). (6) Wake lock while a side plays. The test's header check follows. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24hifi. Next: makers' own masters as works of kind music; a queue across records; Chromecast/AirPlay are the browser's own (the audio element exposes them).
- The platter, the sides, the sleeve (Will, 24 Oct, on the phone: "off-centre symbol"): the platter had collapsed to a dot on a phone (an absolute child in a figure with no width): `.lr-disc` now has a width (min(420px, 100%); 72vw on a phone) and centres. Sides are named as a person says them (`tidyTracks`: underscores to spaces, the prefix every side shares cut away ('Ludwig van Beethoven - symphony no. 5… - ' → 'I. Allegro con brio'), roman numerals up, tempo words up; applied at bring and at render, so older rows read right). The Archive's description comes through a wrong decoding now and then ('1804â08'): `unmoji` mends the common sequences; and when the house has written no 'why', the Archive's own description sits folded under 'From the Archive's record' instead of standing as the sleeve. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24platter.
- The Listening Room as one room (Will, 24 Oct: "how can you make a Spotify-like experience"; "let's keep going"): /listening and /listening/:slug are one document (`shell` in listening.js): the shelf and the record are views (`[data-lr-view]`, `.lr-shelfv`/`.lr-recv`), a record opens over the shelf without a reload (pushState; the address follows; Back and 'The shelf' return), and the player is a bar at the foot that stays (`.lr-bar2`: cover, title and side, previous · play · next, time, the queue; the progress line along its top). `window.__LRA` carries every playing record with its sides (`recordJSON`; GET /api/listening the same). The queue (`Q` in public/listening.js): 'Play it through' lines the record up with the rest of the shelf behind it; 'Play the shelf' starts at tonight's; a side follows a side and a record follows a record with a line ('Next: …', `.lr-toast`); a sheet (`.lr-queue`, Q) shows what is lined up; prev restarts a side after four seconds. The platter turns only when its own record plays (`lr-on` on the view; `lr-going` on the room for the glyphs). Media Session, Best|Light, Wander with it, the dimming and the wake lock as before. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24lrapp.
- Every record has a face (Will, 24 Oct: "what images should we upload as thumbnails, at least temporarily"): none need uploading. `labelSvg(r)` in listening.js draws the house's own record label after the paper labels of the period (a disc with grooves, a coloured label with a gold ring: oxblood, deep blue, forest or black for the shellac, cream for the open repertoire; the composer in small capitals, the title in the serif around the spindle hole, the performer in italic, the year, WATERMARK), served at /listening/:slug/label.svg (and .png by sharp, for the lock screen's artwork, `recordJSON.art`); `cover()` falls back to it, so the shelf, the record, the bar, the queue and the front door's rows always show a record. A held work (`cover_slug`, set on the Studio's desk) or the Archive's picture still wins. The front door's rows: a book without a cover stands in cloth (`cloth()` in rows.js, `.rw-cloth`, the shelf's palette; the caption hidden since the board carries it), and a disc tile shows the whole record with its name beneath (`.rws .rw-disc`). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-24label.
- The maker's desk (Will, 23 Oct: "the back of house as beautifully designed as the front; one screen; anybody could manage it"; built 24 Oct): the top of /account (`desk()` in account-views.js `dashboard`, `.dk2-*`) is the line (today's sales, this month, how many things need you, the key's number) and three columns of rows with one verb each: Needs you (Answer N requests · Answer a claim · Decide on representation · Add your bank when money is owed · Publish N waiting · See Keeping · Grade; 'All clear.' when empty) · The work (Add work · The holdings with held/public/jewels · Your prices · Your page · The edit's standing in one line · Deliver an album) · Money (This month · All time · Owed to you / The statement · Your bank or Add your bank · Royalties · The split); 'Lately:' one line of the last three sales. - CloudFront in front (Will, 25 Oct, "lets do it"; DNS for krafftco.com is a Route 53 hosted zone in his AWS account, so the edge is CloudFront, not Cloudflare): distribution `EUW7MFQLDRUTK` (d1wj8xafp4wtz7.cloudfront.net), pay-as-you-go, origin `watermark-3a4z.onrender.com` HTTPS only (response timeout 60 s), ACM certificate for watermark.krafftco.com (us-east-1, DNS-validated in Route 53), viewers redirected to HTTPS, all methods. Behaviours: Default (*) = CachingDisabled + AllViewer (every page and API passes through with cookies and the Host header, nothing dynamic is ever cached at the edge); `/img/*` and `/static/*` = cache policy `watermark-pictures` (TTL min 0 / default 3600 / max 86400, honouring the origin's Cache-Control; key = Accept header (WebP vs JPEG) + all query strings, no cookies; `private` answers such as the veil are never cached) + AllViewer. WAF off (the house's own limiters; WAF's SQL rules can refuse uploads; ~$14/mo). Error pages: 502/503/504 → `/static/cold.html` (the /offline page served as a static file with `public, max-age=86400`, so the edge holds it through a deploy), caching TTL 10 s. Code: `app.set('trust proxy', TRUST_PROXY || 1)` (Render: set TRUST_PROXY=2 behind CloudFront, else every visitor reads as the edge's address and the limiters bite everyone); analytics reads `cloudfront-viewer-country` (needs the origin request policy AllViewerAndCloudFrontHeaders to arrive). The cutover is the `watermark` CNAME in Route 53 → d1wj8xafp4wtz7.cloudfront.net (CloudFront's 'Route domains to CloudFront' can write it), done with Will watching; back out = point the CNAME at Render again. Render keeps the custom domain as is.
- CloudFront, live (25 Oct, 3:45 pm): the `watermark` CNAME in Route 53 points at d1wj8xafp4wtz7.cloudfront.net (TTL 60 s); checked from Will's Mac: pages and API 'Miss from cloudfront' (never cached), /img a miss then 'Hit from cloudfront', a signed-in POST 204 through the edge, the Studio cookie honoured. Render has TRUST_PROXY=2. A deploy's 502 minute now shows the house's cold page from the edge to everyone. Back-out: the CNAME to watermark-3a4z.onrender.com and TRUST_PROXY=1. Later: the AllViewerAndCloudFrontHeaders origin request policy for the country; WAF if the house needs it; an invalidation (Studio → or the console) if a picture must change inside its hour.

- The clearance desk (Will, 26 Oct: "we can accept this work. The rule, use by use: Collect, a story and a print need no release (a personal copy; news or comment; a work of art); a company and a campaign (COMMERCIAL) need a model release from each recognisable person (signed by a parent or guardian for a child) and a property release when private property or a trademark is prominent. Releases: `work_releases` (kind model|property, subject, signed_on, minor, by, file, mime, kept_at), added on the work's record ('What may be done with it', `block`: Al sees, the table of uses, the releases held, Add a release (the signed form as PDF or a photograph through the bag check 'papers', kept in the vault under `releases/` and DATA_DIR/releases, served only to the maker and the house at …/clearance/releases/:id/file, never shown), the attestation, the house's say); POST /account|studio/works/:slug/clearance do=release|unrelease|attest|override. /clearance: the guide (street photography, portraits, children, property, what each use needs, how to keep the papers); the house's forms /release/model.pdf and /release/property.pdf (pdfkit, drafts for counsel). The API's work record carries `clearance {model_released, property_released, people, uses}`. `release.kept`, `clearance.attested/overridden` hidden on the register. Footer The house → 'What you can do with a photograph'. Counsel: the release wording, the editorial line, the child rule by state. Test: smoke 'the clearance desk'.
- Sharing a book (Will, 26 Oct: "we have to make it where you can share a book"; "easily in feed"; "everything has to be easily shareable"; "every aspect of sharing a book should be something considered and beautiful"; "frictionless, and add it to someone's collection"; "make people feel like they are collecting and have already collected"; "beautiful graphics and heads for our books"): `src/sharebook.js`, `src/ornaments.js`, `public/share.js`. (1) The card: every link to a book unfurls into the house's card (/read/<slug>/card.png, 1200 × 630 by sharp: the binding standing on the dark wall, the title and author in gilt, the line, the wave); a passage link (/read/<slug>?c=n&q=<words>) unfurls into the quote card (?q=: the passage set in the serif on paper, '— Author, Title'); og:image on /read and /library pages (`type: 'book'`). (2) The reader: Share in the bar (`[data-rd-share]`, S) opens the sheet `.rdr-sh` (the book on its card, the passage when one is chosen `.rdr-sh-q`, Send it (Web Share, else Copy the link), the house's letter); selecting a passage on the page raises the pill `.rdr-qp` 'Share this passage'; a link with ?q= lights the passage on arrival (`.rdr-lit`, `light()` in read.js) and a toast says who sent it. (3) The letter: POST /read/:slug/send {to, note, q, c} (a signed-in collector or maker; twenty a day; mail: the card, the note in their words, 'Read it, and keep it on your shelf' → ?keep=1&from=<first name>). (4) The keeping: ?from= puts '<Name> sent you this book' on the cover (`.rdr-sent`); ?keep=1 keeps it on a signed-in reader's shelf the moment they arrive ('On your shelf.'), else 'Keep it on your shelf' → sign in and back. (5) Wander's book screen gains Keep (`[data-st-keep]` → POST /read/:slug/shelf json; a stranger is sent to sign in and the book kept on return) and Share (`share(w)` for kind text); glyph templates plus/share. (6) Everything shareable: `public/share.js` on every page: any `[data-send]` (data-send-title/text/url, else the page's og tags) shares by the device's sheet or copies the link (`.shr-say`); the work page's act row gains Share (`.ap-share`), the Library page's shelf buttons gain Share (`.rl-share`). (7) The ornaments: `ornaments.js` six families of printer's flowers (vine, rule, fleuron, laurel, stars, wave) chosen per book by its hash: a headpiece above every chapter head (`.rdr-hp`) and a tailpiece at its end (`.rdr-tp`, in place of ❦). Found on the way: the binding's SVG ids now carry the width (two sizes of one book on a page shared defs and the cover washed out). `data-send`, never `data-share` (the Collect form's attribute). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26share. Test: smoke 'sharing a book'.
- The press (Will, 26 Oct: "a portal where print shops can receive orders from Watermark and make them; the pipeline for printing absolutely seamless; empower local printers to fill the supply of the demand and establish relationships with their customers"; "we love printing. Benjamin Franklin"; "take over all those print shops in terms of our software so good for it"): `src/printshops.js`, `.pj-*` (the desk), `.prs-*` (the room). A print shop is a maker of kind 'printer' with a `print_shops` row (city, state, zip, address, papers, max_in, framing, pickup, ships, turnaround, story, status open|held, house); the house's own room (Will's PRO-1100, 17 in) is seeded as the first shop (`ensureHouse`, house=1). Every print the house sells (print_orders 'asked' with cents > 0: a proof, an edition, a print asked for and priced) becomes a `print_jobs` row by `sweep()` (after each sale by the minute timer, and from Studio → The press 'Place what is waiting'): `choose(o)` = an open shop whose sheet is wide enough (and frames / ships as the order needs), the collector's state first, then the nearest zip, the fewest jobs; else the house's room. `offer()` mails and notifies the shop (notice kind `print`) with the fee: PRINT_SHOP_PCT (60) of the print's price; the maker is paid from the house's side as before. The shop's desk /account/printing (rail 'The press' via `V.setDeskPrinter`; the house's own room for the house maker): Offered · In hand · Delivered, the shop's settings; /account/printing/:id: the picture, the ticket (`ticket()`: number, size, paper, framed, the pencil line, the master's pixels, the address, the collector's first name, the fee), Accept / Not this one → It is on the press → It has shipped (tracking) → Delivered; the master untouched at …/file (three takes, after accepting; `originalPath`, never re-encoded), the ticket as a PDF (pdfkit) for the bench. `move()` mirrors the step onto print_orders (accepted→asked, printing→making, shipped→sent, delivered→done), writes to the collector at each step naming the shop (the delivered letter links the shop's page: the relationship), tells the work's maker on delivery, and a decline offers the job on to the next shop. The shop's money: `earned(makerId)` / `clearing()` join the maker's statement (payouts.js `printing`, 'Prints made for the house') so Friday pay carries it once delivered and cleared. Public: /printers (the room: 'A press in every town', the shops by state, 'Print for the house' → `printer_applications`; honeypot `website`), /printers/<handle> (the shop: papers, widest sheet, framing, pickup, turnaround, prints made); the maker page's role 'Prints for the house'. Studio → Back office → The press (/studio/printers): applications (Invite → a maker of kind printer + its shop, the invitation letter via `inviteLink`; Not now), the shops (Hold / Open), the benches (reassign to a shop, take back), Place what is waiting; Studio → Prints shows where each print is (`where()`). Register: `printer.invited` ('X, Dallas now prints for the house', Patrons) and `print.delivered` ('A print of X by M was made by Alder Press, Dallas and delivered', Collected) public; print.offered/accepted/printing/shipped/declined/file and printer.asked hidden. Next: the shop's own prices per size, Connect for the shop's bank (it is a maker, so /account/payouts already offers it), the shop's page on the collector's holder's room ('Made by'), a shop choosing a job from a board, proofs by the batch. Test: smoke 'the press'.
- The greats in the stone (Will, 26 Oct: "not make the Franklin connection so obvious, but do it where it is best; we want people inspired by the greats"; "like Old Parkland, if Old Parkland was a website"; Harlan Crow's library; the Perot, named by the children for the parents, its halls carrying the families who gave them; "nothing cheesy, but beautiful"; "make people feel rich, because they are, when they have access to art, culture, books, history"): `src/inscriptions.js`. The rule: a room's lede never names its ancestor; the room carries one carved line, the person's own and out of copyright, signed and dated (`insc(room)`, `.insc`: the serif in letterspaced capitals between two hairlines, the caption in small capitals; gilt on the dark rooms), and the printing house a ring of names in brass (`ring()`, `.brass`: Gutenberg · Manutius · Plantin · Caslon · Baskerville · Bodoni · Franklin · Morris). Lines: the printers' room 'Festina lente' (Manutius, 1501), the printing house Franklin's epitaph ('B. Franklin, Printer', 1728), the Library and the collector's own 'I cannot live without books' (Jefferson to Adams, 1815), the Keys 'hang together' (1776), the desk Poor Richard's, and more in LINES for rooms to come. The ledes of /printers, /press and the franchise lost the name (the franchise test now looks for 'the printer's own'); Franklin's shelf in the Studio and the Writer's Desk epigraph stay, since those are his rooms. Named rooms (after the Perot): `room_plates` in hall.js (room → one line); Studio → Back office → Hall of Patrons 'Named rooms' (POST /studio/hall/plate; an empty line takes it down); `app.locals.roomPlate(room)` draws the brass plate `.rmp` under the room's name on the Library, the Listening Room, the Screening Room, the Keys, the press and the printers' room. The collector's own library: /yours/reading is now the Library's walnut room under their name ('<First>'s library.', the plate 'Founding Collector, No. n · est. year', the Jefferson line, planks Reading now (chapter n of N) · Kept for later · Read, the bindings standing; `shelves.room/plank/book` opened on `app.locals.shelves` with `plate`, `path`, `kicker`, `foot`, `insc`, and a `note` per book). Test: smoke 'inscriptions and named rooms'.
- Signed on the spot (Will, 26 Oct: "the legal way a photographer can open Watermark on their phone and get someone to sign a release immediately with a little gesture, a sign of the finger"): `src/sign.js`, `public/sign.js`, `.sg-*`. The maker opens /account/release/sign (?work=slug; ?kind=person|property; the door on the work's record's clearance card 'Sign a release now, on this phone' (`app.locals.signRelease.block`), and a line in the vault (`line`)) and hands the phone over: the release text (the house's draft forms, the same words as the PDFs), the subject's name (a child: the guardian's name and the child's), an email for their copy, the E-SIGN consent ('I agree to sign this release electronically and to receive my copy electronically; I have read it and I am signing it freely'), the pad (a canvas drawn with pointer events at the device's resolution, smoothed, Clear; the signature leaves as a 900 × 300 PNG in a hidden field; Sign stays disabled until a stroke). POST /account/release/sign: the record as a one-page PDF (pdfkit: the text, the photographer and the work, the name and date, the signature as drawn, an audit line: the moment UTC, the address, the device, the record kept under release No. n) written to DATA_DIR/releases and the vault `releases/`, its sha256 and a token on `work_releases` (new columns maker_id, token, email, sha256, place; by 'signed on the house'), `release.signed` hidden on the register, the subject mailed a link to their copy (/release/r/:token, the PDF, private). A release signed before the photograph is in waits with work_id 0 and is attached from the work's record (POST /account/works/:slug/clearance/attach; the card lists them). The clearance desk reads it as any release, so a company's use and a campaign open at once. Counsel: the wording, E-SIGN/UETA sufficiency of the record (intent, consent, a copy, retention), the child rule by state. Test: smoke 'signed on the spot'.
- The lab (Will, 26 Oct: "let's go hand this off to something and get people's work printed"; the pipeline seamless, with no one at a bench): `src/lab.js`. A fine-art print-on-demand house as one more shop on the press (`print_shops.lab='prodigi'`; maker handle `the-lab`, kind printer, no desk): never on /printers, never for a pickup or a frame, chosen by `choose()` after any local shop that can make the print and before the house's own room (a declined shop is never chosen again for that order: `declinedShops`). When a job is offered to it the house places the order by API (`place`: POST /v4.0/Orders with `X-API-Key`; recipient parsed from the address, US only; one item, `sizing fitPrintArea`, the master by a one-time address /lab/asset/:token (3 days, 5 takes, `lab_assets`); `callbackUrl` /lab/callback?k=<sha of the key>; the pencil line in metadata), records `lab_order`, `lab_state`, `lab_cost` (the lab's charge, the house's own) and moves the job to accepted through the press's `move`, so the collector is written to as for any shop; the callback and a ten-minute `poll()` (`sync(order)`: inProduction → printing; shipments or shipping → shipped with carrier · number · url; Complete → delivered; Cancelled → declined) carry it on. The price first: POST /v4.0/quotes before every order (`quoted` = print + shipping in cents, kept as `lab_cost` when the order carries no charges); a print the lab would make for more than LAB_MAX_COST_PCT (80) of the collector's price is never placed (`lab_state` 'too dear', declined and offered on, the house mailed 'at a loss'); the panel shows Paid · The lab's cost · Kept per job. A failed placement is noted (`lab_tries`, `note`) and tried again by the poll; after three the job is declined and offered on (the house mailed). The lab carries no fee (`feeFor(o, shop)` = 0) and earns nothing on a statement; the letters and the register say "the house's lab", never the lab's name. SKUs: read from Prodigi's catalogue on 26 Oct (the key accepted; all sizes found): Hahnemühle Photo Rag is `GLOBAL-HPR-<W>X<H>` (308 gsm, US, 48–72 h; $13 for 11 × 14, $20 for 16 × 24, $32 for 20 × 30 at the lab), German Etching `GLOBAL-HGE-…` (`familyFor(paper)` picks it when the order names it); `DEFAULT_SKUS` over `SIZES`, overridden by LAB_SKUS (JSON size → sku); LAB_ATTRS (paper/finish attributes), LAB_MAX_IN (36), LAB_SHIPPING (Standard). Studio → The press gains the lab's panel (`panel()`: state, the sizes and their SKUs against the catalogue (POST /studio/lab/check → GET /v4.0/products/:sku, kept in settings `lab_catalogue`), Ask the lab about open orders, its jobs). `on()` = PRODIGI_API_KEY set (PRODIGI_SANDBOX=1 for their sandbox; render.yaml declares both); the shop is held while off. Tests set `app.locals.lab.transport` to a fake wire. mail.js keeps `log` of letters when mail is off (`app.locals.mailLog`). Will's hands: a Prodigi account, the key in Render (sandbox first), the catalogue check green for each size, one sandbox order end to end, then live. Test: smoke 'the lab'.
- The first week (Will, 26 Oct: the Make verb's front end; "make it hum"; the test every outside maker takes, from the invitation letter on a phone to a page, prices, a bank and a paid sale without anyone on the phone with them): `src/firstweek.js`. (1) The letter lands in the vault: a maker's first opening of /account/join/:token with no work goes to /account/upload?welcome=1 (other kinds and returning makers to /account). (2) Seven mornings: `mornings()` (hourly; two minutes after start) sends each maker of kind 'maker' with an email, for days 1–7 after `joined_at`, one letter with the first undone act of seven (`acts(m)`: your work · your page · your best in front · your prices · your bank · your page sent · an album), one tap to its door; never a done act, never twice a day (`first_week` maker_id/day/act; a day with nothing undone is marked 'done'); day one waits for the morning after (20 h), `force` for tests; the house's own maker skipped. (3) Who is stuck where: `line(m)` ('2 of 7 · at your best in front · a day' / 'All 7 done') under each maker's status on Studio → Accounts (`.ac-week`; `where(m)`). (4) The first sale: a listener on `ledger.append` (chained after notify's) on a paid `license.granted` for an outside maker whose paid licences now number one → `maker.firstsale` (hidden) on the register, the house mailed ('X's first sale'; a word from you today) and the house maker told by notice. Words drafts for Will. Test: smoke 'the first week'.
- The jacket (Will, 26 Oct: "think about when someone clicks into a book: how do we sell a book? the most beautiful front and back, even for a book we pulled; everything they need: the author, is this an important piece of writing, did it inspire a film"): `src/jacket.js`, `.jk-*`. Every public book gets the house's own front and back matter, kept on `texts.jacket` (JSON), written by Al as the study is (research with the web search tool: first published where and how, standing, adaptations with years, the author's life; then `write_jacket`, a tool with a fixed schema; `parse` keeps https sources only; JACKET_MODEL else CLOSER_MODEL; `fill()` 35 s after start then half-hourly, JACKET_PER_RUN 20, books first; a miss kept as {missed}). The front (`front(t)`, `.jk-front`, on /library/:slug in place of the summary): the line in the serif, two or three paragraphs, Why it matters, when and where it first appeared, Read it if you…; the back (`back(t)`, `.jk-back`, after the text): the author with dates and other works, Its afterlife (film · television · play · opera · music · influence, with years and a note), In conversation with (companions, linked and marked 'in the house' when the house holds the title: `inHouse`), Read further, 'Written by Al, the house's AI, from research on the web'. The reader's cover carries the jacket's line and 'About this book' (→ the Library page). Studio: on the Library page, Write the jacket / again / Hide / Show (POST /studio/library/:slug/jacket). `text.jacketed` hidden on the register. The Library page's by-line now reads the author as a person says it (`byName`). Test: smoke 'the jacket'.
- The darkroom (Will, 26 Oct: "can we build a photo editor; better than Lightroom; or at least works in a pinch and then better later"): `src/darkroom.js`, `public/darkroom.js`, `.dkr-*`. /account/develop/:slug (the work's maker or the Studio; the door 'Develop it' at the head of the work's record, `darkroom.door`): the picture large on the dark wall; sliders exposure · contrast · whites · blacks · shadows · temperature · tint · saturation · clarity · sharpen · vignette, black and white, straighten (the largest upright rectangle kept, `innerRect`) and crop (drawn on the picture, fractions); B or a press holds the negative up; R resets; Esc leaves. The truth is the server: while a slider moves the page approximates with CSS filters; at rest (450 ms) GET /account/develop/:slug/preview?r=<recipe> renders the same recipe with sharp from a 1600 px working copy of the original (`base`, cached by sha), so what is seen is what will print (`apply(input, recipe, {width})`: the tone sliders composed into one `linear` since sharp keeps one; shadows by gamma; temperature and tint by `recomb`; clarity a wide mild unsharp mask; vignette a radial SVG multiplied). The recipe is kept on `develops` (POST do=save; `recipeOf` reads anything safely). 'Make it the picture' (do=apply; a flat recipe refused) renders the recipe on the original at full size (JPEG 100, 4:4:4) and makes it the master through `replaceMaster` (the slug, deed, licences and entries unchanged; the walls, prints and deliveries follow), keeping the original once as the negative (`works.negative_file`, under originals/ and vaulted at negatives/); 'Back to the negative' (do=negative) makes the negative the master again. `work.developed` / `work.undeveloped` hidden on the register; `work.master` as for any new master. JSON answers with `json=1`. Not yet: highlights and a tone curve (sharp has no curve; a raw pass is the way), raw files (DNG), masks, a histogram, presets. Test: smoke 'the darkroom' (a stop under is darker; black and white; a crop to a quarter; developed, the wall darker, the negative kept and put back; strangers and other makers refused).
- The darkroom, second pass (Will, 26 Oct, "lets go"): (1) Highlights (−100..100, negative recovers, positive opens, as a photographer expects) and Shadows (now −100..100) are a true tone curve: `lut(r)` builds a 256-entry table (a soft-edged mask on the bright half and the dark half, the middle untouched, the ends never crossing) and `curved(pipeline, table)` runs the pixels through it in one raw pass between sharp's two stages (colour before, detail and vignette after); a picture over 10 MP goes through in bands via temp files and sharp's `join` (`pipeline.tmp` swept by `sweepTmp` in the preview and apply routes), so a sixty-megapixel master never sits raw in memory twice (24 MP in ~2 s). `upright(meta)` gives the dimensions after the EXIF turn (metadata() reports the file's own), used by the crop, the straighten and the bands. (2) The histogram: `[data-dkr-hist]` drawn by darkroom.js from each preview as it lands (luminance on a log scale, R/G/B lighter beneath; 'Blacks held · 0.8% whites clipped', gold when over half a percent). (3) The lab's caption: a lab cannot write the pencil line, so `captioned(file, o)` in lab.js hands the lab the master extended by a white band (a sixteenth of the height) carrying 'No. 3 · The Staircase · Will Fullerton · Florence, 2025' in the serif at the left and the wave at the right; the picture's pixels above it untouched; LAB_CAPTION=0 turns it off. Test: smoke 'the lab' (the band under the picture, white).
- The Wander standard and its walk (26 Oct): `docs/WANDER.md`, ten sentences Wander is held to (opens on a picture; whole and true; one finger moves, two fingers look; nothing counts you; stillness honoured; every screen a record; Collect quiet; left and right land somewhere; no connection is not the end; no faults), and `scripts/wander-walk.js` (Playwright, `PW` names the module, `WALK_BASE` the house, default the dev house on 3081): a phone (iPhone 13) and a desk (1440 × 900 at 2×) walk that checks each sentence (screens in the HTML, scrollWidth, true size and object-fit, the rail's words with no digit, Collect last and no wider than 1.3× the widest act with no fill, the toggle and the record's pill both ways, the still state at 6 s and its waking, a synthetic one-finger drag = one screen, a pinch holds and a flick lets go, the doors by swipe and by arrow, the pack after 8 s and the wall growing offline with the toast, script errors and failed requests) and prints `wander: all ten hold` or the faults by number. A change to Wander ships only when it does. The live host is not reachable from the cloud shell (the proxy's allowlist), so the live walk runs from a Mac. Found on the way: Collect was 1.7× the rail's weight (min-width 44/48, letter-spacing .04em, padding trimmed: now 55 px against Bravo's 45).

The front door's What this is carries five doors (`.wh-5`: Look · Read · Listen · Collect · Make; three across under 1100px, stacked on a phone). The collection page now passes path '/collection' to layout (it had passed '/'). Tests follow ('the menu, in order: the five verbs').

Taylor's and Harlan Crow's kind): `src/collectorhouse.js` (`head(o)`), `.ch-*`, docs/HOUSE.md 'The collector's house'. The head of /yours (Gallery tab, when there is a piece) and of a public gallery /g/:code: the best piece large on the dark wall (own hand first, else the first piece), 'Your house' / 'A collector's house', the name, the plate (the first collector or Founding Collector No., the collector's key, pieces by makers and of their own hand, earliest number, collecting since, the eye's line), the collector's statement in italic (or a one-line form to /yours/statement when none), the piece's caption; and for the owner four rows of doors: Show it (Wander your gallery · Your address or Open the doors · On your television · In a headset · Hang a wall · A show, by invitation), Live with it (Print it, the first unprinted piece · The proof · Instax · The holder's room), Keep the record (The papers · The cabinet · The catalogue · What it is worth), Grow it (A piece a month · Your looking · The basket · The Keys).
