- The piece finds its gallery (26 Oct, the sign-in Jobs would have: no form between a stranger and their first piece): on /thanks a Collect paid with an email that already has a gallery (a buyer with that email who has a code, a name or a piece) hangs in that gallery (`licenses.buyer_id`) instead of a fresh one for the device; the tile says so (`.yx-theirs`: 'It hangs in the gallery of x@… Sign in once on this device and it is all here', the link to /signin?next=/yours); the device is never signed in as them on an address alone. A first-ever email still gets its gallery and the cookie at once, as before. (The links in tours.js and guests.js use sign-in's `next`, not `back`.)
- Sideways on a bright picture (Will, 26 Oct, a pool in Mallorca on the phone: "I can't see buttons in this view, plus the shield is hiding in the top"): in the landscape block of style.css each act of the rail sits on a frosted disc (44 px, rgba(11,10,9,.45) + blur; Collect 52 px with the wave and the price only), and the shield (`.st-eye`) moves out of the bar's row to under the wordmark at the left, on its own disc. The rules stand at the end of the landscape block so they win. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26side.
- No settings (Will, 26 Oct, the Jobs cut): Wander's Sit is an act, not a sheet: the More menu's Sit sits you down at once (90 s a work, the guide read, quiet music, the wake lock) and 'Stand up' brings you back; the settings sheet (`.st-ss`) is never opened (its markup stays, hidden). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26sit.
- Wander, on your phone (26 Oct, the Jobs cut: the first run ends with the app on the home screen): in public/stroll.js, on a phone in a browser (never the installed app), from the third visit (`wm_visits`), on the sixth arrival (`installTick` from `arrive`), one card `.st-install`: Install where the browser offers it (`window.__wmInstall` from layout's beforeinstallprompt), else on an iPhone 'Tap Share, then Add to Home Screen' with How → /app; Not now puts it away for good (`wm_install_told`); it leaves by itself after fourteen seconds. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26install.
- The raw door (26 Oct, the darkroom's negative): `src/raw.js` reads a DNG's TIFF structure by hand (IFD0, chained IFDs, SubIFDs; `isDng` by DNGVersion 50706; `previews` = every JPEG inside (Compression 7/6 with Photometric 6/2 at 8 bits, or JPEGInterchangeFormat), largest first; `unwrap(file, {min 1600, to})` writes the largest out as a JPEG; `NO_PREVIEW` the plain word). Nothing decodes the sensor data (no LibRaw on the box). The bag check knows a DNG (type 'dng'; the preview must decode; none → turned away with the way to make one: Lightroom's JPEG Preview: Full Size); `app.locals.raw.unwrap/keep` in app.js: takeWork (accounts.js) and takeMaster take the preview as the master (originalname .jpg) and keep the DNG as `works.raw_file` under originals/ and the vault's negatives/ (`<slug>-raw-<sha10>.dng`), `work.raw` hidden on the register; a known or smaller outcome drops the DNG. Tests: `test/raw.js` (in npm test; a DNG built by hand) and smoke (a DNG through /account/works/one; a tiny preview refused).
- The house from inside Wander (Will, 26 Oct: "you have to be able to access Read and Listen"): the Yours door (swipe right) opens with the five verbs (`.sd-verbs`: Look · Read (Your library with n books open when reading, else the Library) · Listen · Collect · Make (a maker's studio, else For those who make)), for strangers and the signed-in alike.
- Write, the app (Will, 26 Oct: "let's copy Obsidian"; "we should be able to have that in Writing or as the Writer's Desk"; "think of it as a standalone app that works perfectly in Watermark"; "a series of powerful creative tools under Watermark: someday a movie editor, painting, drawing"): the Writer's Desk as an app, in src/writersdesk.js and public/writersdesk.js. (1) The door: /write (a stranger is told what it is with Sign in; a maker lands in the last manuscript). (2) The manuscript is a vault of Markdown files: Export (/account/desk/:id/export.zip: folders as folders, each page `NN Title.md` with front matter title/kind/status/synopsis/updated, a README; open it in Obsidian) and Bring it in (POST /account/desk/import, a folder of .md/.txt with `paths` beside each file (the desks page's form sends webkitRelativePath), the vault's root dropped, front matter read, into a new manuscript or `into` one). (3) On the page: Read (⌘E, a reading view: heads, emphasis, lists, quotes, code, [[links]] that open or make the page, #tags), Open (⌘O, a quick switcher by a few letters, fuzzy; a new title makes the page), ⌘P (the palette: every act by name), Search (⌘F; GET …/search?q= over title and body with a snippet and a count; a tag in the inspector searches it), Graph (⌘G; GET …/graph: pages as points, [[links]] as gold lines, a folder's pages ringed; a small force layout on a canvas; drag; tap opens), Today (⌘D; POST …/daily: today's page in the Journal folder, once a day), the inspector's Outline (the heads of the page; tap scrolls to it) and Tags. The page's JSON carries `tags`. Test: smoke (the door, /write, search, graph, daily, export's zip read with unzipper, import with front matter, the link round-tripped). sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26write. Next tools under the house (Will): painting and drawing (the easel is the seed), the movie editor someday; each a verb with its own door, its files yours to take out.
- Draw, the app (Will, 26 Oct: "painting, drawing"; the second creative tool under Watermark, after Write; the easel is the seed): `src/draw.js`, `public/draw.js`, `.dw-*` (checked: no room had it). /draw is a blank sheet on the dark wall for anyone; /draw/from/:slug draws from a held work (a mature work sends to its door; ?back=): the picture beside the sheet on a desk (Reference, R) with the easel's tools over it (Grid G, Values V, Squint S, Notan N, a link to the easel), or under the sheet at a quarter to trace (Trace, Shift+T); on a phone Reference shows the picture over the sheet (a tap returns). Brushes: Pencil (P, graphite, lighter with a light hand, a grain), Ink (I, thickens as you press), Brush (B, soft round), Wash (W, wide and thin, opacity 35, builds only across strokes: a stroke is drawn on its own canvas and laid onto the layer at the tool's opacity when the pen lifts), Eraser (E), Hand (H); pressure from a pen; Shift holds a straight line; [ ] size; a palette of twelve and any colour. Layers (up to 24: add ⌘L, hide, reorder, opacity, rename by a double-click; a drawer on a phone, 'Layers'), undo/redo of the active layer's pixels (⌘Z, ⌘⇧Z, thirty deep), two fingers or space or the hand pan and zoom (the wheel too; Safari's gesture refused), Mirror (M), Fit (0), the screen kept awake, a fit again on load and when the stage resizes. The drawing is yours: autosaved to the browser every ten seconds (`wm_draw_<key>`); PNG takes the flattened picture; File takes the house's own file (.wmd: a JSON of layers as PNGs, title, size, the reference's slug) and Open brings a .wmd back or lays an image in as a layer; Keep (⌘S; a maker or a collector; a stranger is sent to sign in) saves both under your name (`drawings` table; DATA_DIR/drawings/<id>.wmd and .png, copied to the vault's `drawings/` when S3; POST /draw/save takes the body as its own type `application/x-watermark-drawing+json` so the house's 64 KB form and JSON limits do not apply, DRAW_MAX_MB 40); /draw/mine (rail More → Draw) lists them; /draw/d/:id opens one again with its reference; /file and /png serve the owner only. A maker's 'To the vault' (POST /draw/d/:id/vault) makes it a work of their own through `registerWork` (kind 'drawing', kept back as 'hidden' until published; `drawing.vaulted` hidden on the register). Doors: the work page's why line ('Draw from it'), the easel's bar ('Draw'), Wander's More (Draw now opens the sheet, not the easel). Nothing of a drawing is told to anyone. Walked with Playwright at desk and phone size (scratchpad draw.js, dw2.js, dw3.js: a stroke lands, undo and redo, a wash on a second layer, the eraser cuts, a pinch zooms without touching the pixels, Keep and reopen). Test: smoke 'Draw'. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-26draw. Next: a filmstrip of recent drawings under the sheet; brush presets saved; a timed sitting as on the easel; the drawing beside the reference in Wander as a screen; the movie editor someday.
- The darkroom, third pass: looks and masks (Will, 26 Oct, "let's go"): (1) Looks: `LOOKS` in darkroom.js, eight of the house's (Film · Rich · Soft · Portrait · Landscape · Silver · Platinum · Noir, drafts tuned by eye), as chips at the head of the panel (`.dkr-looks`, `[data-dkr-look]` carries the recipe); a look sets light and colour and leaves this picture's frame and masks alone; the maker's own: 'Keep as a look' with a name (POST do=look → `develop_looks` (owner, name, recipe without crop, rotate or masks); dashed chips `.dkr-look-m`; Forget this look, do=unlook, the owner only). (2) Masks, local adjustments: `r.masks` (up to four; `masksOf`): a gradient (`linear`: whole at x0,y0, gone at x1,y1, smoothstepped) or a circle (`radial`: cx, cy, rx, ry in fractions, feather f), `inv`, and its own `adj` (`MASK_ADJ`: exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows, temperature, saturation). Rendered by the house pixel by pixel: `rawPass(pipeline, fn)` is the one JavaScript pass (the curve's `curved` is now a case of it; bands through temp files past 10 MP), `masked()` renders each mask's adjustment on the band (`localAdj`: linear, recomb, modulate, the curve's table) and blends it in by `maskAt(m, x, y)` (the weight at a point); after the curve, before detail. The panel: A gradient / A circle, drawn on the picture (a drag; the red wash `.dkr-mk`, an SVG overlay in fraction space, shows the one being set; a tap on a mask's card selects it; Invert; Remove; Esc deselects), each with its own sliders (`.dkr-mask`, `[data-dkr-masktpl]`), the hidden `masks` input; `isFlat` counts only a mask with something set. Found on the way: sharp's `stats()` reads the input, not the extract (the smoke now extracts to a buffer first); an SVG element's `hidden` must be set by attribute. Walked with Playwright (scratchpad dkr3.js, dkr4.js). Test: smoke 'the darkroom'. Next: a brush mask (painted on the darkroom's canvas, carried as a small PNG), a luminance range mask, the looks on the Library's batch bar (one recipe over many works).
- A look over the archive (26 Oct, the Library's batch bar): the bar at /account/table gains Look… (the house's eight and the maker's own, 'yours') and Develop: POST /account/works/batch do=look look=<key|mine:<id>> saves the look on each selected work (its own crop, straighten and masks kept: a look is light and colour) and queues it in the darkroom (`enqueue` in darkroom.js: one at a time in the background, `state(slug)` waiting | developing | done | failed, `queued()`), the toast 'N developing in the darkroom; the walls follow as each is done' with Undo → do=negative (back to the negative, each that has one, `putBack`). The room's own acts are now the shared `develop(w, r, who)` and `putBack(w, who)`; a develop always renders from the negative (kept once, the first time), so looks never compound, and the room's preview base reads from the negative too (`negativePath`). Test: smoke (the darkroom block: queued, developed, back).
- The second shelf (Will, 26 Oct: "more titles for books"): `CANON` in reading.js is 270 titles (was 60), set out by room in the file (fiction, poetry, drama, history and lives, ideas, faith, science, children); a hinted number is checked by `verify` before it is trusted, a null is found by title and author, and 'Shelve the canon' on Studio → The public domain skips what is held, so a second run brings only what is new (one every 1.5 s; no pause under test). Will: press it once after this deploys; the misses are named on the desk.
- The second shelf's misses (27 Oct, the first run on the live house: 108 new books by the 185th title, the misses all titles Gutenberg names its own way or files under no author): `CANON` entries may carry a fourth element, aliases (Father Goriot; Notre-Dame de Paris; Fathers and Children; Shakespeare's Sonnets; On the Origin of Species), tried in turn by `find`, `verify` and `shelvedBy`; an author of 'Anonymous' (Beowulf, Roland, the Rubáiyát, the Gita) asks nothing of the record's author line; the drama, the children's shelf, the lives and the ideas that the search missed carry hinted numbers (verified before trust, as always). Never push while a canon run is on: a deploy restarts the house and ends the run.
- The pictures in the books (Will, 27 Oct: "how can we also figure out pictures in these books"): `src/bookplates.js`. Gutenberg's plain text drops the plates; its HTML edition keeps each picture in its place (Tenniel's Alice, Rackham, Doré, Pyle, Beardsley, Shepard). `bring(t, rec)` reads the HTML edition (`htmlUrl` from the record's formats, else /ebooks/<id>.html.images), takes every picture over 220 px a side (ornaments, rules and the Gutenberg logo left behind; BOOK_PLATES_MAX 80, 6 MB each) into DATA_DIR/texts/<slug>/pl-<n>.jpg (1600 px, JPEG 90) and the vault `texts/<slug>/`, reads its caption (figcaption, a caption paragraph, else the alt), and places it by the words right after it found at the head of one of the book's own paragraphs (`place`: the reader's chapter and paragraph, 'before'; else the words before it, 'after'; else unplaced, hung at the end as 'The plates'); kept on `texts.pictures` (JSON), `texts.illustrator` the credit (the Studio sets it on the Library page). The reader sets each figure between the paragraphs (`.rdr-fig`, PIX in the /read route), the plates at the end of the last chapter (`.rdr-endpl`), a strip on the cover (`.rdr-cover-pl`, 'N pictures in the text · Illustrated by …'); the Library page carries the strip (`.lb-plates`) and, for the Studio, 'Bring the pictures' / 'Read the book for its pictures again' and the illustrator. GET /read/:slug/pic/:n serves one (a day's cache). A book shelved from Gutenberg queues its pictures at once (`queue`, one at a time in the background); Studio → The public domain 'Bring the pictures' reads every held Gutenberg book without them (`queueAll`). The book's cover stays the house's binding everywhere. `text.illustrated` public on the register. Test: smoke (the reading room block: two plates in, the ornament refused, the first set before the chapter's first paragraph with its caption, the second at the end, the strip, the credit, a stranger refused).
- Feeds in (Will, 27 Oct: "how is this getting us ready for the Met and all of the other people? when they give us more material, we can show it; we're training for ingest of all sorts of different media"): `src/feeds.js`, the bulk door. Any institution's whole feed as one background job: read into items of the IIIF importer's shape and each taken through the same door (`iiif.take`, lifted out of iiif.js's run: the picture fetched, the bag check, `registerWork` as the maker's, the licence and the year), so no second road. Readers: a CSV by address or file (`csvItems`: title, image, source, place, date, medium, description, artist, kind, credit; its own `parseCSV`), OAI-PMH Dublin Core (`oaiPage`: ListRecords, resumptionToken paging, the first identifier that is a picture or a IIIF image service, deleted records skipped), a IIIF collection (its manifests walked) or manifest, the Met's API, a list of books (Gutenberg numbers or 'Title, Author' a line → the reading room's `bring`/`find`). `feeds` keeps every run (status queued | running | done | failed | stopped; looked, taken, skipped, failed; `missed` JSON with why and the item, up to 500; `cursor` so a restart resumes; `retry_of`); FEED_MAX (2000) a run, FEED_PAUSE_MS between pages. Studio → Back office → Feeds in (/studio/feeds: the form, the runs refreshing themselves while live, Stop; /studio/feeds/:id: the counts, the misses with why, 'Retry the misses' (a new run over the saved items), What came in). `collection.imported` on the register with `feed`. `app.locals.feeds` {enqueue, retry, kick, run, pages, parseCSV, csvItems, oaiPage, kindOf, missesOf}; `app.locals.iiif` gains parseMet, isMet, take, doFetch. Next: a watched bucket or Dropbox folder as a feed; a document kind (a PDF or page scans as a book); audio guides as text plus a recording; the rights desk's conditions carried per feed. Test: smoke 'feeds in'.
- The document kind (Will, 27 Oct: "we are training for ingest of all sorts of different media"; the exhibition catalogue, the finding aid, the monograph): `src/documents.js`. A PDF, or page images in order, becomes a `texts` row of kind 'document': every page kept as a picture (`texts.pictures` with `page: n`, 1600 px, in the vault under texts/<slug>/), the text read from the file's own layer (pdftotext) or by OCR (tesseract) where a page has none (DOC_OCR_PAGES 400; DOC_MAX_PAGES 600), set into paragraphs (`paragraphs`: hyphens joined, running numbers dropped) and chapters the reading room's way, each page placed at its chapter and paragraph; `texts.doc` JSON (pages, text layer|ocr|mixed|none, words, sha256, pdf); the original kept byte for byte at documents/<slug>.pdf (and the vault) and served at /read/<slug>/original.pdf. The reader (reading.js): the text with a Pages toggle (`.rdr-pgtog`, ?pages=1: one page a screen, `.rdr-pgf`), the cover 'N pages · M chapters' with the first pages fanned; a scan with no text opens on the pages. Doors: Studio → Back office → Documents (/studio/documents: a PDF or page images, or a PDF by address; the queue in the background), Feeds in kind 'documents' (a CSV with a `pdf` column: title, author, pdf, year, source, terms; auto-read from the header). Dockerfile adds poppler-utils and tesseract-ocr. `texts.doc` and `texts.lang` are created with the table in library.js (the ALTER of a later module runs before the table exists in a fresh house). Next: hOCR (Internet Archive, BHL) and ALTO (Gallica, LoC) as the text instead of OCR when the source has them; EPUB/XHTML (Standard Ebooks, Gutenberg EPUB3, Wikisource); TEI (EEBO-TCP, Perseus). Test: smoke 'the document kind'.
- The Archive's own reading (27 Oct): `bringIA(id)` in documents.js takes an Internet Archive text item as the record (metadata API), the PDF (format 'Text PDF' first) and the Archive's aligned OCR (`_djvu.txt`, a page per form feed) in place of the house's own, so nothing is re-read; the rights read from the item (possible-copyright-status NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT, licenseurl publicdomain/CC0/BY/BY-SA, rights 'public domain') and a closed item refused with the reason; terms credit the Archive's scan. Doors: the Documents desk ('an Internet Archive item'), Feeds in kind 'ia' (an advancedsearch or scrape address, paged; or identifiers a line). The surveys of 27 Oct (pictures, texts, sound and film) are in the Krafft project as 'The World's Shelves'. Sherlock Jr. is held (`hold` in screening.js FIRST; the seed shelves it and clears its showings): every score on home video for it is modern and in copyright; Sunrise's Movietone score (1927, mono) fell with the film. Rule for the door: a stereo track on a pre-1929 film is a modern score.
- Audio guides as objects (Will, 27 Oct): `src/audioguides.js`. A spoken guide is a recording and its words, on a work, from whoever made it (a museum's curator, the maker, a guide of the house): `audio_guides` (work, maker, title, speaker, role, lang, file in the vault under guides/, mime, bytes, seconds, sha256, transcript, license, source); the bag check reads the bytes; an .srt/.vtt becomes paragraphs at gaps over two seconds (`plainTranscript`). Served with ranges at /guide/:id/audio (a day's cache when the work is public), the object at /guide/:id.json; shown under the work's Look closer (`block`, `.agd`, each with its player and 'The words'); Wander's guide sheet plays the first one when the house has no reading of its own (`first` → `wanderJSON.audio`/`guide`, `spoken` = who). Doors: the maker's desk /account/guides (rail More → Spoken guides; the file, the work by slug/accession/source address, who speaks, the words as text or an .srt/.vtt file; Remove), Feeds in kind 'guides' (a CSV: work, audio, transcript (text or an address), title, speaker, role, lang, license; auto-read from an `audio` header). `guide.spoken` public on the register ('The Met added a spoken guide to X, in Y's voice'). No museum publishes its guide audio openly (the survey), so this is the house's format, offered through the rights desk. Test: smoke 'audio guides as objects'.
- Each item's own rights (27 Oct, the survey's caution: a feed mixes open and closed objects; Te Papa, Wellcome, Europeana): `rightsKey(s)` in iiif.js reads a Creative Commons or rightsstatements.org address or words into the house's keys (cc0, pd, cc-by, cc-by-sa, cc-by-nc; 'closed' for ND, in copyright, all rights reserved, undetermined, or anything it cannot read as open). Every reader carries `rights` on the item (CSV column rights/licence/edm:rights; OAI dc:rights; a IIIF canvas's or manifest's rights/license), and `iiif.take` sets `works.open` from the item first, the feed's licence only when the item says nothing; a closed item comes in with `open` null and the statement kept on `works.rights_note`, so nothing is offered until a person says. `work.opened` by:'item'. Test: smoke 'feeds in'.
- The inbox (Will, 27 Oct: a watched folder as a feed; how a registrar works day to day): `src/inbox.js`. A folder the house watches for a maker or an institution: a prefix in the house's own bucket (inbox/<handle>/; the local vault under test) or a Dropbox folder (DROPBOX_TOKEN in the environment; list_folder / download / move_v2). Every INBOX_EVERY_MIN (5) and on 'Look now': each new file through the same doors as a feed (a picture → `iiif.take` with `it.file` as the maker's work, with its sidecar's record and rights; a PDF → `documents.take`; a recording → `audioGuides.keep` on the work the sidecar names); sidecars `index.csv` (a row per file: file, title, place, date, medium, description, artist, kind, credit, rights, work) or `<name>.json` beside a file, an .srt/.vtt/.txt beside a recording as its words. Taken files leave the folder (deleted in the bucket, moved to Taken/ in Dropbox); refused ones stay, named on the desk with why, never looked at twice (`inbox_seen` by key and size). Studio → Back office → Inboxes (/studio/inbox: watch, Look now, Pause). `collection.imported` with feed 'inbox'. localStore gained `download`. Test: smoke 'the inbox'.
- The readers' feed and the commonplace book (Will, 27 Oct: "a readers' feed; what is the short form of a book; snippets and paragraphs"): `src/commonplace.js`. The short form of a book is the passage; Franklin and Jefferson kept them in commonplace books. /wander?in=shelf is Wander for words: one passage a screen on the paper (`.st-i.st-pas`, `passage` in stroll.js's textCard and public/stroll.js's screenFor), chosen so it reads whole (`whole`: 180–720 characters, begins and ends like a sentence; `passage(seen)` over public books, essays, documents; `passages(seen, n)` never twice), the book standing small beside it; 'Open the book here' lights the passage (/read/<slug>?c=&q=); Keep (`[data-st-cp]` → POST /commonplace/keep, the words checked against the book, so every entry is a true quotation; a stranger is sent to sign in) copies it into `commonplace` (buyer, text, chapter, para, passage, note); Share sends the quote card's link. /yours/commonplace: the book (`.cpb`), each passage to its place, a line of your own under it (POST /commonplace/:id/note), Take it out. /api/stroll?in=shelf feeds more. Doors: the Library's lede, Yours → Settings beside Your reading, the Wander drawer's Read verb. Test: smoke 'the readers’ feed and the commonplace book'. sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-27shelf.
- Standard Ebooks (27 Oct, the survey's first target for texts): `src/standard.js`. The finest editions of the public domain, taken from their GitHub source (raw.githubusercontent.com/standardebooks/<repo>/master/src/epub/): `parseOpf` (title, author, the abstract, LCSH subjects, the Gutenberg transcription it was made from, the spine), `parseChapter` (the heading's parts joined 'II: The Road'; every <p> with emphasis kept as *word*, abbreviations unwrapped; <hr> a break; each <figure>/<img> placed before the paragraph it precedes), front and back matter left out (SKIP). Illustrations drawn by sharp (SVG at density 200, on white) into texts/<slug>/pl-n.jpg as the book's pictures. When the same book stands here from Gutenberg (dc:source's number, else title and author), the Standard Ebooks text takes its place at the same address (slug, reading list and commonplace kept; `text.reedited` public); else shelved new (`text.shelved` from 'standard-ebooks'). Terms name the edition; the reader's edition line reads 'The Standard Ebooks edition'. Doors: Studio → The public domain 'Standard Ebooks' (an address → POST /studio/reading/standard), Feeds in kind 'se' (addresses or repos a line; a single address; or the whole catalogue through api.github.com's org listing, paged, resuming at the cursor; a 403/429 names the rate limit). Test: smoke 'Standard Ebooks'. Note: standardebooks.org and api.github.com are not reachable from the cloud shell; live only.
- Smithsonian Open Access (27 Oct): Feeds in kind 'si' (`siItems` in feeds.js): an api.si.edu search (the key from the address or SI_API_KEY, declared in render.yaml), paged by start/rows; only CC0 records with a picture, the IDS file at its largest (deliveryService with no max), the maker from the freetext names, date, medium, place, credit, unit and accession as meta, the record's own page as the id; rights CC0 on each item. The National Museum of Asian Art and twenty other units through one door. Test: smoke 'Smithsonian Open Access'. DROPBOX_TOKEN declared in render.yaml too.
- Wikimedia Commons (27 Oct, one reader for the dozen museums that publish only there: Nationalmuseum Sweden, the Walters, the National Library of Wales, the Mauritshuis): Feeds in kind 'commons' (`commonsItems`): a category's address → api.php generator=categorymembers with imageinfo (url, size, mime, extmetadata), paged by gcmcontinue; the original file, the title from ObjectName else the file's name, the artist and date, the licence per file (LicenseUrl else LicenseShortName → `rightsKey`; a PD-Art painting pd, CC0 cc0, an NC file cc-by-nc: shown, never sold). Test: smoke 'Wikimedia Commons'.
- The canon's last sixteen (27 Oct): `verify` falls back to the text's own header when the record cannot be read or reads as another title: `headerRecord(id)` fetches the .txt.utf-8, reads 'Title:' and 'Author:' from the Gutenberg header (the surest record there is), and keeps the text (`rec.raw`) so `bring` never fetches it twice. Hints and aliases for Plutarch (14033, 'Plutarch's Lives, Volume I'), Caesar ('De Bello Gallico and Other Commentaries'), Vasari (25326), Teresa, the Cloud, Julian.
- The Library of Congress (27 Oct): Feeds in kind 'loc': a loc.gov collection or search address (?fo=json, c=100, sp= paged, access-restricted:false; 3.5 s between pages for the Library's 20 a minute); each result with a PDF becomes a document through `documents.bringUrl` with the Library's record (title, contributor, date, rights, description, the item's address as source). Selected Digitized Books: 90,000 free-to-use American books. Test: smoke 'the Library of Congress'.
- The governor (Will, 27 Oct: "are we overwhelming the website? we need to be resourceful"; the house went down (503, the cold page) with two chores pressed at once on the 512 MB box: the pictures of 192 books and the whole Standard Ebooks catalogue): `src/governor.js` (`app.locals.governor`, mounted after guardrails). One rule for every heavy job the house runs for itself: `turn(label, fn)` is one lane, one item at a time across every job (feeds in, the pictures in the books, documents; the illustrations of an edition ride inside the feed's turn); `settle(label)` waits between items: a floor of GOV_PAUSE_MS (600), then while memory is over GOV_MEM_MB (380, rss) or more than GOV_BUSY (6) visitor requests landed in ten seconds (static files and /img not counted), at most a minute. Visitors are never governed. A feed cut by a restart resumes two minutes after the house is up (was 15 s), `feeds.restarts` counts the restarts, and at two the job is 'paused' with a note instead of resumed (it may be what brought the house down); Resume (POST /studio/feeds/:id/resume; also for stopped and failed) puts it back at its cursor. The pictures queue lives in memory and is simply gone after a restart (press it again). /healthz (full) `governor` {rss, limit, pressure, busy, visitors10s, lane, waited, held, load}. Rule: never press two heavy jobs on the live house at once; one, and watch /healthz. Test: smoke 'feeds in'.
- The edge after a boot (27 Oct: the house was back and sound at the origin while watermark.krafftco.com kept answering 503 'caught a cold' from CloudFront for twenty-five minutes, Age 1500 s on the error): `edge.everything()` invalidates `/*`; the house sends it 25 s after start when CLOUDFRONT_DISTRIBUTION is set (`[edge] cleared after boot`), and POST /studio/edge/clear does it by hand. Will's hand, once: the distribution's custom error responses (502/503/504 → /static/cold.html) should carry an Error Caching Minimum TTL of 10 s (the AWS console session had expired, so it was not checked today). Check the edge with `x-cache` and `age` on the answer: 'Error from cloudfront' with a large Age means the edge, not the house.
- Durability (Will, 27 Oct: "build for durability; the most stable system, but allow us to continuously innovate; a development room constantly running; we have to outrun everybody"): docs/DURABILITY.md. Built: `CHORES=0` (governor.chores(); feeds, the pictures and documents pumps stay off) as the kill switch; `/healthz` brief carries `ver` (the commit's first eight) and `chores`; `.github/workflows/watch.yml` (every ten minutes: the origin and the edge, four doors; a failed run mails the repository's owner by GitHub's default; the edge failing while the origin is sound is named as the edge's fault) and `live.yml` (after `test` passes on main: wait for `ver` to match at the origin, up to forty minutes, then open twelve doors through the edge); `scripts/rollback.sh` (revert HEAD and push); `render.staging.yaml` (the development room: service `watermark-next` on branch `next`, STAGING=1, starter, its own disk; applied by hand, never synced). The flow once Will opens the room: build on `next`, walk it there, promote to `main` by merge once or twice a day. Next, in order: Postgres (zero-downtime deploys; the chores on a worker), the worker, a second origin.
- The showing (Will, 27 Oct: "when Ross Perot and his team, the Met and the heavy hitters are ready, our thing must be ready"): one switch on Studio → Launch (POST /studio/showing, `settings.showing`; `app.locals.beta.showing()`): on, every chore of the house holds (`governor.hold`; `chores()` false), the house desk's line says 'Being shown · chores hold · no deploy', and the rule for the people is no push until it is off; off, the chores resume. Before any demonstration: the showing on, /healthz read, the live walk green, no push. Test: smoke (feeds in block).
- A shipped product (Will, 27 Oct: "we built a product and it is a legitimate shipped product; we've done the MVP and now every launch is adding on; we'll run it and sharpen it"): docs/PRODUCT.md (the sentence, the release discipline, the team). `package.json` 1.0.0; `CHANGELOG.md` (## <version> · <name> · <date>, then lines; 1.0 · The house opens · 27 October 2026); `src/releases.js` reads it (`releases()`, `current()`), /releases shows it (`.rls`), the footer's line carries the version linking there (`RELEASE` in views.js) and About's papers line gains What's new. Versions: the second number for a room or a promise, the third for repairs, the first when the house changes shape (Postgres + the worker = 2.0). Git tag v1.0.0.
- The gate was red (27 Oct, found by the live walk's 'skipped'): `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` had failed on every push of the day (body-parser GHSA-27r3-jxmh-p828, multer GHSA-q7qp-rjg7-q26m; both fixed by `npm audit fix`, express 5.3.0 / multer 2.5.0 / body-parser 2.4.0), so `live` never ran, and Render deployed anyway (its 'Wait for CI' is not on). Rules: after every push read `gh run list --limit 3`; a red `test` is a stop. Five pushes in forty minutes this morning each restarted the house (a minute of the cold page each): batch, and once the development room is open, promote once.
- The gate, green at last (27 Oct, 1 pm; the site read as down at 12:57 while the audit-fix deploy's cold minute landed): every CI run since the gate was built on the 22nd had been red: audit first, then `npm test` stopping at 'poppler on the box' because the GitHub runner lacks what the Dockerfile gives the house; test.yml now installs poppler-utils and tesseract-ocr first. A commit whose message carries `[skip render]` is not deployed by Render (nothing restarts), and live.yml walks the house as it stands instead of waiting for a version that never comes; use it for workflow, docs and CLAUDE.md pushes. First green test and first green walk: 27 Oct, 13:15 ET. Will's switch 'Wait for CI to pass before deploy' is now safe to turn on.
- The bare page (Will, 27 Oct, 1:14 pm, Wander on his phone with no stylesheet: a blue wave, a blue ×): during the deploy's cold minute the edge answered /static/style.css?v=… with the cold page as 200 HTML, and the worker's background refresh kept it as the stylesheet. Three layers now: sw.js keeps a /static answer only when its type fits the file's name (`fits`, `KIND`) and a picture only when it is an image, a wrong answer never replacing a good copy (`V` = wm-2026-10-27fits); every page checks after load that `--bg` is set and, if not, drops the caches and reloads once a session (`wm_heal`, in `layout`); the watch and the walk refuse a door whose body is the cold page even at 200. The edge was cleared by POST /studio/edge/clear. Will's hand in CloudFront, once: the custom error responses (502/503/504 → /static/cold.html) must answer with the real code (503), not 200, and cache for 10 s; a 200 cold page is what every cache downstream keeps.

The mark `texts.edition='jewel'` with `edition_no` (never reused), `edition_intro`/`edition_intro_by`; made or taken off on the Library page's desk (POST /studio/library/:slug/edition do=make|unmake) or from 'The twelve' on Studio → The public domain (`TWELVE`: Franklin, Alice, Grimm, Paradise Lost, the Inferno, Walden, Leaves of Grass, Meditations, Moby-Dick, Treasure Island, The Wind in the Willows, Pride and Prejudice; found on the shelf by title and author; the illustrator and where the plates are, as a note). Plates by hand (POST …/plates): a line each, an address or a Commons File: name (read through Commons' API with the artist and licence as the credit) | the chapter (blank for the end plates) | a caption, through the bookplates pipeline (`by:'hand'`, `credit`). Read aloud (POST …/read-aloud): every chapter without a reading queued through the governor's lane, `speech.render` (now exposed) a chapter at a time into `readings` (text, chapter, hash, file under readings/ in the vault), served at /read/:slug/audio/:c, offered at the head of each chapter in the reader (`.jl-play`), and kept as a record in the Listening Room (`recordings` source 'reading', slug reading-<slug>, a chapter a side, the binding as the sleeve, the shelf 'Read aloud'); a missing voice stops the queue and says so. Commons is not reachable from the cloud shell (live only). Test: smoke 'editions of the house'.
- The net under the house (27 Oct, 3:30 pm: the origin flapping, answering then unreachable for seconds at a time, Studio pages timing out, the pictures queue emptied again and again; a crash loop was the likeliest reading): server.js catches `unhandledRejection` (logged with its stack, the house goes on) and `uncaughtException` (logged; only a second one inside a minute exits, and Render brings it back); both counted in `process.faults`, shown on the brief /healthz as `faults` {rejections, exceptions} beside `up` (seconds since start, so a crash loop is visible from the street: `up` never grows), and in full on the Studio's report. Next: the boot chores (warmAll, backfill, jacket and speech fills, seeds, warmAlbums, recovery) through the governor's lane and `chores()`, so a restart is quiet.
- The quiet boot (27 Oct, after a day of restarts: a deploy's cold minute and then a dozen chores firing inside the first minute on a half-CPU box; Render's Events showed no crash, only deploys): `governor.boot(label, fn, ms)`: every chore the house runs for itself at start (warmAll, the closer, jacket, speech and art fills, the shelves backfill, the Listening and Screening seeds, twins, letters again, warm albums, the first conservation check, the pictures resume) is scheduled through it: nothing before BOOT_QUIET_S (180), then one at a time BOOT_GAP_S (15) apart in the order registered, each through the lane, skipped when the chores are held; `state().boots` lists them. The governor's memory line follows the box: 40% of the cgroup's limit (/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.max), else 380 MB, GOV_MEM_MB overrides. The pictures run survives a restart: `settings.pictures_run` set when the shelf is pressed, cleared when the last unread book is read, taken up again at boot; a book with no HTML edition (or a 404) is marked read with none (`pictures='[]'`) so the run never circles back to it. Render: the plan is Pro (2 CPU · 4 GB, 27 Oct); Auto-Deploy set to 'After CI Checks Pass'. Test: test/boot.js (in npm test) and smoke (the pictures block).

(2) The binding's wave was the old stroked sine laid on the fillet, so it read as a mark cut by a line: binding.js now draws the logo's own wave (`I.WAVE_D`, exported from icons.js) in gilt, whole, inside the frame above the inner fillet. Test: smoke (the reading room block).

Both in `rechapter` and at bring.

Each has one scoped IAM user: `watermark-vault` (S3 on the one bucket; AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID/SECRET in Render) and `watermark-edge` (CreateInvalidation on the one distribution; CLOUDFRONT_ACCESS_KEY_ID/SECRET, read by src/edge.js, else the AWS pair). Swapping the AWS pair for a key from the other account makes every deploy die at `checkKey` ('Access Denied', exit 1) while the running house keeps serving; the Render log's `[edge] cleared after boot <id>` proves the edge's key, `[vault] cors ok` the vault's. The vault account's root keys are deleted and MFA on; `watermark-edge`'s unused key 1 is Will's to delete. Letters: the bank connected (payouts.js `bankTold` on account.updated with payouts_enabled, once per account: bank name and last four from the event) and a studio's other addresses changed (accounts.js profile). - The morning digest and the alarm (27 Oct, 1.2.1; SECURITY.md's second item): `src/digest.js` (`app.locals.digest` {compose, digest, alarm, wrongs}), mounted after the backups. `digest()` once a day from DIGEST_HOUR (13 UTC; `settings.digest_day`; checked every ten minutes, first look two minutes after start) mails HOUSE_EMAIL `compose()`: THE WEEK (analytics' 7d: visitors, pages, stood, hats, bravos, collects and dollars, first-time collectors, sign-ins, new makers, asked; yesterday), WHAT WENT WRONG (5xx and 404 counts, `process.faults`, the last five errors from analytics' memory), HOW FAST (p50/p95 pages and API, the slow pages), THE KEEPING (register, works, vault unvaulted/lost/last error/cors, backups and the drill, fixity, conservation and the mirror, mail sent/failed, stripe, voice, guide, signing, the governor, disk and db), NEEDS THE HOUSE (requests, unpaid, asking to hang, applications, prints, claims, feedback). `alarm()` every five minutes, sent at most once an hour (`settings.alarm_at`) and only with `wrongs()`: more than ALARM_5XX (20) server errors in the hour, a fault inside the hour, the register failing to verify, a stale backup, a lost original, mail failing inside the hour. `house.digest`/`house.alarm`/`device.ended` hidden on the register. Studio: GET /studio/digest (the letter as text), POST /studio/digest/send and /studio/digest/alarm by hand. Under test (`noTimers`) nothing is scheduled; with mail off the letter is logged `[digest]`. Test: smoke 'the digest'.
- The development room, open (27 Oct, 9:47 pm, "lets built it"): Render service `watermark-next` (srv-dbgl85uc6qcc73c06mp0; branch `next`; Oregon; Starter; disk 5 GB at /data; health check /healthz; STAGING=1 and the other non-secret values of render.staging.yaml; SESSION_SECRET and VAULT_KEY generated) made by hand from the dashboard, never a blueprint. Secrets are Will's to add in its Environment (ADMIN_PASSWORD, MAIL_ALLOW, RESEND_API_KEY, test Stripe keys, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET); the CNAME next.watermark in Route 53 too. The flow from here: build on `next` (push `next` alone; Render deploys it after CI), walk it there, promote to `main` by merge once or twice a day. `next` is no longer reset to `main` after each push; it leads.
- Two engines, one shape (Will, 27 Oct, 10:30 pm: "The database"; "Let's do it"; "whatever is best for platform and companies" → Render Postgres): `src/pgsync.js` (the bridge: a worker thread holds the one connection, the main thread waits on shared memory with Atomics.wait, so `db.prepare().get/all/run`, `exec`, `transaction` keep SQLite's shape on Postgres; `src/pgworker.js`), `src/dialect.js` (SQLite → Postgres by rule: `?`→`$n`, every datetime/date/strftime/julianday modifier (`wm_mod()` on the server for a modifier built at run time), INSERT OR IGNORE, pragma_table_info, sqlite_master, LIKE→ILIKE, IS ?, MAX/MIN two-arg → greatest/least, total, group_concat, json_extract, instr, @named, camelCase aliases quoted, DDL INTEGER PRIMARY KEY → identity and INTEGER → BIGINT), `src/db.js` chooses by DATABASE_URL (`db.kind` 'postgres'|'sqlite'; `db.url`). Hand fixes in words both accept: INSERT OR REPLACE → ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE (settings, mirrors, translations, inbox_seen), upserts qualify the table (`views.count+1`), aliases out of HAVING/WHERE, SUM(a=b) → SUM(CASE), COLLATE NOCASE → lower(), DISTINCT+ORDER BY → GROUP BY, `hw.rowid` → work_id, `WHERE 0` → `1=0`, house_asks' missing status → answered_at. `scripts/sql-check.js` prepares every `.prepare(`/wrapper statement in src against a real Postgres (2,444; 0 faults; concatenated and `${}` statements listed as dynamic). Backups (backup.js): pg_dump custom format, the drill restores into `wm_drill_<n>` and drops it (needs CREATE DATABASE; else the archive is listed and said so); `scripts/restore.js` fetches a .pgdump.gz and names the pg_restore; Dockerfile adds postgresql-client-17 (PGDG). `scripts/to-postgres.js <watermark.db>` moves a house (schema by the app, tables in FK order, sequences set, counts compared, the register verified). CI (`test.yml`): branches main and next; a `postgres` job (postgres:16 service) runs the smoke, sql-check and vault/drop/beta/fee/boot with the schema dropped between. `test/dialect.js` in npm test. /healthz `db`. Local Postgres for the shell: /var/lib/wmpg (port 5433, socket /tmp; `su postgres -s /bin/bash -c "/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin/pg_ctl -D /var/lib/wmpg/data -l /var/lib/wmpg/log -o '-p 5433 -k /tmp' start"`; URL `postgres://postgres@localhost:5433/wmtest?host=/tmp`; drop the schema between runs). Rules: a new statement must pass sql-check; never `INSERT OR REPLACE`, never an alias in HAVING, never a bare integer as a boolean, never `SUM(a=b)`. Next: Render Postgres for the test house (DATABASE_URL), then the live move by to-postgres.js with the showing on.
- Postgres client = server version (27 Oct, CI's first Postgres run): pg_restore from a newer client writes `SET transaction_timeout` (17+) and a 16 server refuses it (exit 1, "errors ignored"); the Dockerfile's `ARG PG_VERSION=16` and CI's PGDG install pin the client to the server's version (watermark-next-db is 16; change both when the server moves). CI's log storage is blocked from the cloud shell, so the postgres job's `run()` emits a failure's last 1,500 characters as a `::error` annotation, read with `gh api repos/krafftco/watermark/check-runs/<job id>/annotations`. CI runs on pushes to `next` as well as `main` (it had only watched main, so the development room was deploying untested).
- The test house on Postgres (28 Oct, 7:15 am): `watermark-next` runs on `watermark-next-db` (Render Postgres 16, Oregon); `/healthz` says `db: postgres`; the house made its schema on the empty server and refilled its holdings from the Studios feed; every public, Studio and account door answers; the first `pg_dump` into the vault and the restore drill (a scratch database on the same server, the register matched and verified) both pass on Render. The live move is `docs/MOVE.md`, a runbook for one evening: the live Postgres at 1 GB RAM or more, PG 16, Oregon; the showing on; the copy from the service's Shell (only the Shell sees the disk); DATABASE_URL; one deploy; the disk kept a week, then removed for zero-downtime deploys. Fixed on the way: the house desk's Needs the house listed Settle / Renew / Complete twice.
- The live house on Postgres (28 Oct, 7:46 am ET; Will: "do the build now"): `watermark-db` (Render Postgres 16, Oregon, 0.5 CPU / 1 GB, 15 GB, dbname watermark). The copy ran from the live service's Shell (Will's hand: the Shell is a production door the guard keeps me from) as `FIRST_COLLECTOR= DATABASE_URL="$MOVE_DATABASE_URL" CHORES=0 NO_SEED=1 node scripts/to-postgres.js /data/watermark.db`: 148 tables, 133,171 rows, the register verified, 60 s; then DATABASE_URL set and one deploy; the register's head was 4384 before and after, so nothing landed in the gap. `MOVE_DATABASE_URL` stays in Render until the disk is removed (a week; docs/MOVE.md). Found on the way: founders.js read `settings` at mount before the table existed (FIRST_COLLECTOR set on live only; fixed); the bag check's desk at /studio/door shadowed the Studio door since 21 Oct and looped signed-out visitors (now /studio/bagcheck, with a test); and the backup ran synchronously, so a 151 MB pg_dump froze the house for half a minute and the edge showed the cold page (backup.js now runs pg_dump, pg_restore, gzip and gunzip off the event loop). The dump is 151 MB because of `hits` (90 days); the drill on it is to be run at night, not in the day. Next: the week's watch, then the disk off (zero-downtime deploys) and the worker (2.0).
- The worker (28 Oct, 1.4; Will: "lets continue tightening"): `src/role.js`, docs/WORKER.md. `ROLE` unset = the house as it always ran (the live house today, every test); `web` serves people and keeps only what must live beside them (settleAll, the arrivals pump, vaultPending, push, the balcony, its own warm: `GOV.boot(…, { always: true })`); `worker` runs every chore (every chore timer in src asks `ROLE.chores`; the governor's `chores()` folds it in; feeds are polled every minute on a split house and the pictures run is raised by the web and taken up by the worker; the showing is re-read every half minute on the worker) and writes `settings.worker_beat` every 30 s, which the web's `/healthz` reports as `worker {seen, stale}` and watch.yml fails on when stale. Hand-pressed documents, read-alouds and re-cuts still run in the pressing process (the second cut). `test/role.js` (in npm test). Opening it is Will's hand from the dashboard (WORKER.md); never a blueprint sync on the live house.
- Closed for the moment (28 Oct, the gap the move exposed): `settings.readonly` (beta.js; `app.locals.readonly`, cached 3 s), Studio → Launch 'Close for the moment' / 'Open again' (POST /studio/readonly). On: a middleware right after guardrails answers every POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE from the street 503 (the sign 'Closed for the moment' for a page, JSON for /api, /stripe and the beacons, Retry-After 120), the webhook too (Stripe retries for days), the governor's chores hold; GET pages serve as before. `house.closed`/`house.opened` hidden on the register. For a move, a restore, a migration: minutes. Test: smoke (the showing block).
- The books (Will, 28 Oct: Zeni keeps the books for Krafftco LLC, the Texas entity, the entity in use; "spin it up so that we can get them everything we need"): `src/books.js`, Studio → House → The books (/studio/books?m=YYYY-MM). Three files a month and a one-pager: `/studio/books/<ym>.csv` every money event one a line (date, kind, revenue_class collect | license | print | delivery | tip | subscription | book | liability | payout | cost, ref, stripe_id (session, subscription or transfer), gross, house, owed_to maker:<handle> | collector:<code> | <seller>, owed_name, owed_amount, payer, work, note), from licences (gross = price; house = price − `FEE.makerOf`), tips (the house keeps none), friends/keeping, the Club, franchises, pocket top-ups (a liability), paid proofs, Bookshop copies, Library texts, maker_payouts and pocket cash-outs (negative; they clear the liability), the lab's costs (negative); `/<ym>/summary.csv` the month by class; `/<ym>/balances.csv` what the house holds for others now (`payouts.statement(m).owed` per maker, pocket balances, the Club's prepaid year, recurring friends; take it in the first days of the next month since the tables carry no history); `/how.txt` how Watermark makes money in one page (BOOKS_ENTITY names the entity). Nothing here moves money or calls Stripe. Zeni's side (Will's hands): read-only Stripe team invite, Plaid bank feeds, cards, W-9s for anyone paid by hand; the Stripe account is Krafftco LLC's. `.bks-*` classes (`.bk-*` is the basket's). Test: smoke 'the books'.
- The vault takes a city's dump (29 Oct, Will's Philadelphia: 363 sixty-megapixel masters, 12.4 GB, from TRIP_OUT_EAST_EXPORTS/USA/03_PHILLY/LARGE; Chrome died with 'Aw, Snap' on choosing, before a byte was sent): (1) the vault page drew an `<img>` of the whole file for every file chosen; now each row's picture is decoded straight to 160 px by `createImageBitmap(f, {resizeWidth})` into a canvas, one at a time, only as the row nears the screen (IntersectionObserver), the bitmap closed at once (200 such files: Chrome 404 → 458 MB). (2) Once, never twice (Will: "we do not want you to be able to hit put in vault twice"): Send runs once (`go.dataset.sent`), and POST …/arrivals/start hands back the same arrival for the same file (by, purpose, name, size, within a day): still uploading → the same id (`again`), arrived, being read or held → `already` and nothing is sent (public/arrivals.js `onAlready`). (3) Same size is a sibling, not a copy: `upgradeIfHeld` and `mergeTwins` treat a dhash match as the same picture only when the sizes differ by half again (≥ 1.5× pixels replaces the master, ≤ 1/1.5 is declined as a lesser copy); near the same size it comes in as its own work, so the next frame of a scene is never refused as 'already held'. Test: smoke (the maker's arrivals, a better file on top: a sibling frame).
- Imports, the proof, and the fire drill (29 Oct, Will: "we never can mess up; someday we're going to be entrusted with big, big imports; this has to be seamless"): (1) The proof: the browser fingerprints every file before it leaves (public/sha256-worker.js: SHA-256 written out, read in 4 MB slices in a worker, ~115 MB/s; `WMArrivals.hash`), sends it with the arrival's start (`sha256`), and the queue (`takeOne` in src/arrivals.js) fingerprints the bytes that landed before anything is kept: a mismatch keeps nothing ('The bytes that arrived are not the bytes that left your computer… send this file again'), a match sets `arrivals.verified` and the vault row's step reads Verified. (2) Ask before sending: a start whose fingerprint the house already holds (the maker's own works for a vault; any work for the masters desk) records a done arrival (outcome 'held', verified) and sends nothing. (3) Imports: `src/imports.js`, table `imports` (by, purpose, maker, label, place, kind, manifest [[name,size]…], n, bytes, finished_at, told_at): the vault page makes one per batch (POST …/imports as `application/x-watermark-import+json`, so 20,000 files ride past the 64 KB form limit) and every start carries `import_id`; /account/imports and /studio/imports list them; …/imports/:id (refreshing itself while open), .json, /receipt.csv say each file's state by the latest arrival of the same hand, name and size: not sent yet · sending · waiting · being read · held (verified) · already in the vault · needs you (with why); finished when every file has an answer (or a day on with nothing moving), then a notice (kind `import`) and a letter. (4) The vault page sends three files at a time (3 parts each), each fingerprinted a file ahead. (5) The second look: the 64-bit hash only finds candidates; `images.look`/`alike` (32×32 grey, mean removed) decide whether two files are one picture (SAME_LEVELS 6) before `upgradeIfHeld` replaces a master or declines a smaller copy, and before `mergeTwins` retires anything; when in doubt a file comes in as its own work. (6) The fire drill: test/drill.js, a real import through the real doors (60 MP masters with grain, near-alike pictures, the same bytes twice, a truncated JPEG, a page named .jpg, bytes changed on the way, a connection dropped halfway (S3 road), a file never sent), the receipt checked file by file, every held original checked against the bytes sent, the house's memory and the time bounded; `.github/workflows/drill.yml` runs it nightly (07:30 UTC), on demand, and on pushes to next that touch the import road, on the local road and against moto (an S3 emulator; MinIO's images went behind a login). Its first run caught two different pictures refused as 'already held' (5). Test: smoke (the maker's arrivals: imports and the proof).
- Next for imports (docs/IMPORTS.md): the bucket-to-bucket door for institutions, the Mac uploader (.dmg), one decode per arrival, S3 versioning and object lock on the vault (Will's hand).
- Send from your computer (29 Oct, 1.6.0; Will: "let's start development, by using my archive as what we build it from"): `bin/watermark.js` (one file, Node 18+, no dependencies; served at /push/watermark.js) and `src/push.js` (mounted after the body parsers). `login`: POST /api/push/begin → an eight-letter code and /push?code=, approved where the maker is signed in (POST /push/answer), the command polls /api/push/poll and is given a key `wmk_…` once (`push_keys` keeps its hash and the maker's `sess`, so 'Sign out everywhere' ends it); the key opens only /account/arrivals and /account/imports (`req.wmPush`, read first by `makerOf`), anything else 403. `push <folder…>`: walks (JPEG, TIFF, PNG, WebP, DNG; dotfiles, Previews and .lrdata left), reads Lightroom's stars (an .xmp beside, else the packet inside; −1 is a reject) and keeps `--stars N` and up, `--places` takes the place from the deepest numbered folder ("08_FLORENCE" → Florence), fingerprints a file ahead, one import, start with the sha (ask before sending), parts straight to the bucket (`--files` 3 × `--parts` 4, each retried, a 403 re-signs) or the body through the house on a local vault, done, then waits for the house's answers and prints the import's page and receipt; `--dry` counts and estimates (`--mbps`). State in ~/.watermark/state/<hash>.json (fingerprints by size+mtime, etags by part), so a second run carries on; caffeinate keeps a Mac awake. /account/push lists the computers (End it). The vault page links it. Test: test/push.js (in npm test).
- A museum for everyone (29 Oct, 1.7.0; Will: "a museum of everybody's work"; "making it where people can upload their work and manage all this is the only way this works"; "not an elitist club that I have to manage"; "instead of followers, collectors"; "follow an artist, a collector and a curator"; "do whatever is best for the company and its long term growth and adoption"): `src/museums.js`. (1) The open door: /join is 'Open your museum' (`joinPage`; posts to /record, own.js), every 'Apply with the work' now reads 'Open your museum'; a recorded maker (`makers.door='record'`) publishes to their own museum up to MUSEUM_WALL (36) on view (`wallLeft`; `mayHang` in own.js now means the wall; batch says 'your museum's walls are full'; /account?need=wall). (2) The halls: `works.hall`/`hall_why`; `hallSql(alias)` = invited makers' work, or a work that earned it; applied in `editSql` (the open Wander), `todays`, the collection page, walk's 'collection', For you's `candidates`, the rows' Latest. `earn()` (every HALL_EARN_MIN 5, chores only): someone else paid for it (`collected`), a public wall not the maker's own (`hung`), BRAVOS_TO_HALL (2) bravos (`bravo`); POST /studio/works/:slug/hall {on} is the house's hand, never needed. Find and the maker's own page show everything on view. (3) Following: `follows` (who b:<id>|m:<id>, kind maker|gallery, target); POST /follow {k} toggles (a stranger gets {go: sign-in}; never yourself), GET /api/follow?k= states, notice kind `follow`; `public/follow.js` paints any `[data-follow]` (`.fo-b`), on the maker page (`makerPage({ follow })`, under the stats) and a collector's house (`hs.follow`, `.ch-fo`); counts are never shown; the maker page shows collectors. /yours/following lists them; Wander's `in=following` (`museums.set`, `feed`: the followed makers' work on view, the followed galleries' public collects and public walls, newest first); the Yours door's Look reads 'Following · n'. (4) CURATOR_PCT is 10 (env), from the house's side. Test: test/museums.js (in npm test; MUSEUM_WALL=3 there) and smoke (the recorded maker hangs their own, not in the halls).
- The maker's colour (29 Oct, 1.7.1; Will: "the best export that's color accurate and that does not change their vision at all; if they put it in one way, that's them telling us that's the way they want it"; "it has to work better than it's advertised"): `images.gamut(original)` ('p3' when the master carries an ICC profile that is not sRGB, read with its UTF-16 nulls stripped; else 'srgb'); every rendition (`derivative`, both formats) and `copy()` go out with `withIccProfile(gamut)`, so a P3/Adobe RGB/ProPhoto master is shown in Display P3 with the profile attached (it had been converted to sRGB and clipped) and an sRGB master stays sRGB; JPEGs carry only the house's copyright EXIF via `withExif` (the old `withMetadata` copied the original's EXIF, GPS included, onto every public copy). Renditions are named q4 (all rebuilt), sw.js `V` = wm-2026-10-29colour. `warmSoon(slug)` (app.js) makes a work's six copies the moment `registerWork` or `replaceMaster` finishes, in a lane of its own; `warmAll` now covers kept-back work too (the Library shows it). A 60 MP master: thumb 0.5 s, all six 7 s. The standard: docs/PICTURES.md (what holds, the house's look, next: a soft-proof for the maker, HDR gain maps, AVIF, srcset, tiles in each work's own shape). Test: test/pictures.js (P3 stays P3 and reads the same as the master through sRGB; sRGB stays sRGB; no GPS on a copy; the delivered copy keeps the colour).
- Theirs, said everywhere (29 Oct, 1.7.2; Will: "we want to retain their copyright and their content credentials"; "build in the Leica content credentials"): `images.setOwner(slug → {maker, year})` (set in app.js) and `rightsOf(original, slug)`: every JPEG copy carries EXIF Copyright and Artist, the master's own (Lightroom's) when it has them, else '© year Maker. All rights reserved.', with 'Held on Watermark; license at the source.' after; renditions q5. `credentials.inspect` now finds the camera that signed at capture through the chain (a Lightroom export keeps the camera's manifest as an ingredient): `camera_signer` (Leica, Sony, Nikon, Canon, Fujifilm, Hasselblad…), `captured` true when either; the work page shows `camTag` (`.cc-sig`, 'Signed at capture … signed by Leica at the moment it was made') above the Reference line. The master itself is never touched, so the camera's manifest stays unbroken in the vault; the house's own copies cannot carry the camera's signature (any resize breaks it) and carry the house's signed credentials only once C2PA_CERT/KEY are set. Test: test/pictures.js (copyright and name on a copy), test/museums.js (the line on the page).
- From where you edit (29 Oct, 1.8.0; Will: "whatever is best for artists and the world and adoption from Leica and Lightroom"; "how can we someday integrate with Adobe and Capture One"): docs/INTEGRATIONS.md. (1) `images.saidOf(file)` (exifr xmp+iptc) → {title, caption, keywords, place (sublocation, city), rating}; `inspect` returns it as `said`; `registerWork` fills title (then `fallbackTitle`, the file name, from takeWork), place and caption only when the vault gave none, and keywords/stars (a −1 rating flags 'reject'). (2) `lightroom/watermark.lrplugin` (Info.lua, WatermarkPublish.lua the Publish Service, WatermarkAPI.lua the house calls, json.lua): pairs through /api/push/begin|poll (key in LrPasswords), sends each rendition by /account/arrivals/start then PUT …/body, waits on …/state?ids=, records the slug as the published photo id; a re-publish carries `replaces` (arrivals column), which the queue sends to `app.locals.newEdit` (the maker's own work only; any size; `work.edited` on the register). replaceMaster now names masters `<slug>-master-<sha10>` so earlier ones stay in the vault. Served zipped at /lightroom/watermark.lrplugin.zip; /lightroom (also /capture-one, /send) says how. `test/lightroom.js` runs the plug-in's house calls under Lua 5.1 with test/lr/stubs.lua (curl); CI installs lua5.1. Not yet tried in Lightroom itself: Will's Mac first.
- Pairs (29 Oct, 1.9.0; Will, sending the Paris masters over their web copies: "maybe files are not perfectly the same; this is how we have to figure"): `src/pairs.js`, `.pz-*`, /account/pairs. After an import finishes (`imports.check` → `pairs.soon`), every half hour for makers whose last arrival is ten minutes old (chores only), or on 'Look again' (all), each new work kept back (nothing sold, hung, 60 days, `id` past `settings pairs_last_<maker>`) is compared with the maker's older works of clearly different size (≥1.5× pixels either way; one size = burst frames, never a pair): a grey 160 px copy (from the thumb), normalised correlation across crops in either direction (`score`; coarse 16-grid first over every older work, full 40-grid on the best six), and the camera's moment (`taken_at` to the second). Certain (same moment ≥ PAIR_CERTAIN .9, or ≥ PAIR_CERTAIN_ALONE .97) → `join` at once by the house; likely (≥ PAIR_LIKELY .72, or same moment ≥ .5) → proposed, the maker told (notice) and asked side by side (Same picture / Not the same); the desk's Needs you says 'Confirm N pictures'. `join` keeps the older record, makes the larger file its master through `replaceMaster`, fills its empty caption/keywords/place and the higher stars from the newer, retires the newer as 'merged' (`work.merged` by house|maker); `undo` restores the old master fields from `pairs.prev` (the vault keeps every master), the newer's status, and drops the caches (`work.unmerged`). Table `pairs` (maker, new, old, score, same_time, status proposed|joined|dismissed|undone, how, prev). The scan yields to visitors between pictures. Test: test/pairs.js (in npm test).
- Hang your best, and child safety (29 Oct, 1.10.0; Will: "we need to put my best work up"; people work too, "even without a release for some street photography"; "everybody but [one person] is good to put up"; "maybe some of the kids: just a check"; "prevent bad photos from going up"; "screen and scan and make sure things aren't child pornography"; "don't get sued for child or user accounts"; "show artists what they can do with it: fine art yes, a brand campaign no, someday a portrait in an art book"): (1) `src/hangbest.js`, /account/hang (?from=3|4|5; the desk's The work row 'Hang your best'): the maker's kept-back graded work sorted in the background (`plan`, `start`, Al reads up to 40 unread first) into Ready to hang (no recognisable people, sound file) → 'Hang these N' with the attestation; With people (Will's words on what each may and may not be used for) and With a child (tap to keep back, hang the rest); Not yet read; Look at these first (`qc` on works from the thumb: under HANG_MIN_MP 12, soft under HANG_SOFT .45 of the maker's median Laplacian spread, over HANG_CLIP .3 clipped black or white, a burst twin by the same second and `pairs.score` ≥ HANG_TWIN .9 with the best-graded kept clear) stays down unless tapped in; Kept back. `works.keep_back` (POST /account/hang/keep {slug,on}; keeping a public work back takes it down): refused by batch, the single publish (need=kept), the cull's return, a curator's approved show and the house's first show. The attestation now reads 'these are mine and I hold the rights (showing needs no release; a company's or a campaign's use waits for one)'. (2) `src/safety.js`, docs/CHILD-SAFETY.md: art.js's reading gains `safety {nudity, sexual}`; a child with either, or a refusal to read (`stop_reason` 'refusal'), → `quarantine(w, why)`: status 'quarantined' (status_before kept), keep_back, `safety_holds`, the house mailed without the picture, `work.quarantined` hidden; /img answers 404 for it; adults read as nude go to zone 'mature'. /studio/safety (the house desk's Needs: Decide): no pictures; one recorded look (`safety.looked`); Reported (held, untouched) or Release (`safety.released`). (3) Accounts for adults: /record requires `adult=1` ('I am eighteen or older' on /join and /own; else /join?ok=adult). (4) The kids' corner: analytics counts /kids without visitor, referrer, device, browser or country. Will's hands: PhotoDNA or Safer, NCMEC registration, counsel. Test: test/hangbest.js (in npm test).
- The brief (Will, 29 Oct: "make it so brands can easily work with creators and artists; the contracts, the whole brand partnership thing, we've solved"; "delivery of reels"): `src/briefs.js`, `.brf-*`, 1.11.0. /brands (footer The house → For brands; 'Commission <first>' on every maker page → ?to=<handle>): company, who, email, what, the brief, photographs | a reel | other, how many, uses (commercial, campaign), exclusively, where (us|world), how long (12|24|0 months), budget, date → `briefs` (ref BR-YYYY-NNNN, token), the brand mailed its desk /b/<token> (no account), the artist (if named) mailed and notified. Artists: /account/briefs (open calls and those to them), answer with a fee (≥ $50), a line, up to six refs → `brief_answers`. The brand chooses → `agreement()` (commission and licence from the brief, a draft for counsel, sha256 on `briefs.agreement_hash`) → Checkout for FEE.total of the fee (metadata.brief; `app.locals.briefEvent` in the webhook; /b/:token/paid settles; in test without Stripe it funds at once) → commissioned, the money held. Delivery on the artist's brief page: vault works ticked (a company's or campaign's use refused by `clearance.blocks` until the releases are in) and files (bag check `deliverable`: images, MP4/MOV, WebM, PDF; BRIEF_MAX_MB 4096; DATA_DIR/briefs and the vault `briefs/`) → `brief_files` with fingerprints. The brand looks (inline), asks for a change (back to commissioned with the note) or approves; BRIEF_APPROVE_DAYS (14) quiet → approved (`quiet()`, hourly, chores). Approved: the files are the brand's to take, the fee on the artist's statement (`briefs.earned/clearing` in payouts.js, 'Commissions') for Friday pay. Register: `commission.made` / `commission.approved` public, the artist named, never the brand. Kill fee BRIEF_KILL_PCT (50) is in the agreement; ending by the brand is by hand for now. Studio → /studio/briefs. Test: test/briefs.js. Next: the kill fee as a button; seats for the brand's team; usage reports; the brief's licence as an ordinary `licenses` row so /verify and the API know it.
- The disk kept clear (29 Oct, 1.11.1; Milan stopped at 'ENOSPC: no space left on device'): every rename of the renditions (q2…q5) had left the old generations on the disk, four copies of every picture beside the Paris 60 MP masters. `images.sweep(cache)` drops every rendition not of the current generation (`GEN` in images.js: change it with `fileOf`'s name) and temp files an hour old; `images.free(dir)` (statfs); `clearDisk()` in app.js at start (3 s) and every 15 min (not under noTimers); under DISK_FLOOR_GB (3) free the originals cache trims to half its budget and `warmAll`/`warmSoon` wait. /healthz brief `disk {freeGB, low}`. Rule: a new rendition generation is a disk event; the sweep handles it, but check /healthz disk after.
- No room is never a failure (29 Oct, 1.11.2): arrivals.js `NOROOM` (ENOSPC / no space left), on the error of a direct (bucket) arrival → `wait(a)`: back to 'arrived' with 'Waiting for room…', `clearDisk()`, the pump held a minute; the pump never takes while `diskLow()`; at start, failed direct arrivals with ENOSPC from the last three days go back to the queue (the bucket still holds them: the object is deleted only after a taking in). Via-house arrivals cannot be recovered (their body was cut) and stay failed.
- The lock (Will, 29 Oct: "give these people the redundancy and safety they need"; "we are solving this once and for all"): storage.js s3Store `lock` and `retainAll()`. With VAULT_LOCK_DAYS (Render; 365) and Object Lock on the bucket, puts under originals/ negatives/ music/ documents/ releases/ carry ObjectLockMode GOVERNANCE + RetainUntilDate (+ SHA256 checksum); a refusal sets `lock.state='refused'` and writes plain. POST /studio/vault/lock lays retention over the existing current versions (paged, 20 ms apart; `vault.locked` hidden). /healthz full `vault.lock`. GOVERNANCE, never COMPLIANCE: the account owner must be able to lift it for a court order or a child-safety takedown; the app key never gets s3:BypassGovernanceRetention. The rest is in the AWS console (watermarkventures, bucket watermark-vault-collection): versioning, Object Lock (irreversible), lifecycle (abort incomplete multipart 7 d; arrivals/ noncurrent 7 d), replication to a second region in Glacier Deep Archive. The app key needs s3:PutObjectRetention and s3:GetObjectRetention.
